\mathcal{X}
could be outside
\mathcal{X}
. The use of a semi-distance, which better describes the correlation between 2 features (as expressed by the kernel), also leads to difficulties. We need to ensure that
\mathpzc{d}
is compatible with the Fisher-Rao distance. This is detailed in the paragraph below.

Compatibility between the Fisher-Rao metric and the semi-distance

For
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
and
R>0
, we denote

B_{\mathpzc{d}}(x,R)\coloneq\{x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}\>:\>\mathpzc{d}(x,x^{\prime})\leq R\}

and

\mathring{B}_{\mathpzc{d}}(x,R)\coloneq\{x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}\>:\>\mathpzc{d}(x,x^{\prime})<R\}\,.

For
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
and
x_{0}\in A
, we define the set containing all points from geodesics connecting
x_{0}
with points of
A
,

\mathcal{G}_{x_{0}}(A)\coloneq\{\gamma(y)\>:\>\gamma\text{ is a Fisher-Rao geodesic}\,,\>y\in[0,1]\,,\>\gamma(0)=x_{0}\,,\gamma(1)\in A\}\,.

Lemma 5.2

(Fisher-Rao geodesics remain within balls w.r.t.
the semi-distance)
.

Let
r>0
and
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
. Then

\mathcal{G}_{x_{0}}\left(B_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{0},r)\right)\subset B_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{0},r)\,.

The proof is detailed in Appendix
H.3.1
.
This lemma allows us to use the controls we have on the balls
B_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{j}^{0},r)
on the paths of the geodesics between
x_{j}^{0}
and a point in
B_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{j}^{0},r)
. It entails that
\mathpzc{d}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
are in some sense compatible.

To control the certificates, we also require that certain balls (with respect to the semi-distance) around the particles be disjoint.
We restrict the possible values for the variances to establish this property: we need upper and lower bounds to establish a “pseudo-quasi triangle inequality” for
\mathpzc{d}
(see Appendix
H.3.2
).

Lemma 5.3

(Separation of the balls w.r.t.
the semi-distance)
.

Let
r,\Delta>0
. Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}
.
If

\min_{i\neq j}\mathpzc{d}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\geq\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{u_{\max}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}r^{2}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})}}{u_{\min}}(\Delta+r)\>,\>2\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\Delta\right\}+\sqrt{d\ln\left(\frac{u_{\max}^{2}}{u_{\min}^{2}}\right)}\eqcolon\Delta_{\tau}\,,

then the balls
\mathring{B}_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{j}^{0},\Delta)\cap\mathcal{X}
are disjoint, and for all
j\neq i
,
\mathcal{G}_{x_{j}^{0}}(B_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{j}^{0},r)\cap\mathcal{X})
does not intersect
\mathring{B}_{\mathpzc{d}}(x_{i}^{0},\Delta)
.

The proof is given in Appendix
H.3.2
. We stress that this lemma leads to the separation condition in Theorem
5.1
. It is crucial to manage the kernel on the far and near regions according to the local positive curvature assumption.

Non-degeneration of certificates under a minimal separation

Using the compatibility between the semi-distance and the Fisher-Rao metric, and controls on
K_{\rm norm}
, we can show the existence of non-degenerate certificates under some condition on the minimal separation between the particles of
\mu^{0}
. This is what the following theorem entails.

Theorem 5.2
.

Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}
and that
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC (Definition
5.2
) with parameters
s,\Delta,r,\bar{\varepsilon}_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}
.

If
\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j=1}^{s}\subset\mathcal{X}
satisfies
\min_{i\neq j}\mathpzc{d}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\geq\Delta_{\tau}
(defined in Lemma
5.3
), then there exists a
\left(\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{7}{8}\bar{\varepsilon}_{0},\varepsilon_{2}=\frac{15}{32}\bar{\varepsilon}_{2},r\right)
-global non-degenerate certificate
\eta
of the form (
18
).

Under the same assumptions, for all
j=1,\ldots,s
there exists a certificate
\eta_{j}
for the jth near region of the form (
18
), of parameters
\left(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}=\frac{7}{8}\bar{\varepsilon}_{0},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}=\frac{B_{02}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}/16}{2},r\right)
.

Moreover,
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Psi^{*}p
and
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{j}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Psi^{*}p_{j}
where
\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{2s}
and
\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{2}
.

The proof is based on an adaptation of
(
Poon et al. 2023
, Theorem 2)
, and is given in Appendix
I
.

It remains to prove that
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC—this is the purpose of the following theorem, whose proof is provided in Appendix
J
. We give general bounds for
K_{\rm norm}
in dimension
d\geq 1
, and we provide a tighter constant for
\Delta(s)
in the case
d=1
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
s\geq 2
,
d\geq 1
. Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}
and that
\tau\leq u_{\min}
.
Then
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC with parameters
s
,
r=\frac{0.3025}{\sqrt{d}}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)=0.13139
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)=\frac{0.0894}{2d}
, and

\Delta(s)=2\sqrt{11.9+3\ln(d+6.62)+\ln(s-1)}\,.

Moreover, we can take
B_{02}=\sqrt{4d^{2}+10d}
(item 1 of Definition
5.2
).

Remark 5.3
.

When
d=1
, the minimal separation consition can be improved:
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC with the same parameters
r,\bar{\varepsilon}_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}
as in Theorem
5.3
, with
\Delta(s)=2\sqrt{13.88+\ln(s-1)}
(Appendix
J
).

Remark 5.4
.

Note that the assumption on the separation between particles given in Theorem
5.2
depends on
\tau
through the semi-distance. We write
\mathpzc{d}=\mathpzc{d}_{\tau}
to emphasize this dependency. At first sight, the separation condition thus appears to depend on
n
when choosing
\tau^{2}=\frac{2u_{\min}^{2}}{\ln n}
.
However, as
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\mapsto\mathpzc{d}_{\tau}(x,x^{\prime})
is decreasing and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\mapsto\Delta_{\tau}
is increasing, if
\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j=1}^{s}
satisfies
\min_{i\neq j}\mathpzc{d}_{\tau_{1}}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\geq\Delta_{\tau_{1}}
for some
\tau_{1}>0
, then it also satisfies
\min_{i\neq j}\mathpzc{d}_{\tau_{2}}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\penalty\ \geq\penalty\ \Delta_{\tau_{2}}
for
0<\tau_{2}\leq\tau_{1}
.
This motivates the introduction of the following assumption:

\underset{x_{i}^{0}\neq x_{j}^{0}}{\min}\mathpzc{d}_{\tau_{\max}}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\geq\Delta_{\tau_{\max}}\quad\text{and}\quad\forall j=1,\ldots,s\,,\;x_{j}^{0}\in\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}\,,\quad\tau_{\max}\leq u_{\min}\,.

(22)

The recovery guarantees are not expressed using this assumption, but it is useful when thinking about the convergence of the solution towards
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
as
n\to\infty
. The target is fixed, but for a good prediction
\tau
must decrease with the number of observations. The constraint (
22
) provides in this context a condition on
\mu^{0}
that does not depend on
n
.

Controls on the effective near regions

Theorem
5.3
sets the size of near regions we consider for the certificates. We can lower-bound the Fisher-Rao distance with the semi-distance on these regions, allowing us to extend the control of the estimator to near regions of smaller size (Proposition
3.1
). This result is key to provide recovery guarantees for the proximity of
\mu^{0}
and the renormalized estimator
\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}
(
c.f
.
Corollary
3.1
). Its proof can be found in Appendix
H.4
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume that
x,x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
. If
r_{e}\leq\mathpzc{d}(x,x_{0})\leq r=\frac{0.3025}{\sqrt{d}}
, then
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{0})^{2}\geq\frac{r_{e}^{2}}{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}}
with
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}=2.84
.

6
Sparsity of the solution under large sample sizes

The certificates constructed in Section
5.2
satisfy the requirements of Assumption
1
. Handling such certificates allows us to obtain the non-asymptotic recovery guarantees presented in Section
3
. These results are stated in terms of control of the mass in the far and near regions. Nevertheless, they say nothing about the sparsity of the estimator.

In this section, we present results of a different nature. Our estimator is here an exact solution
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}
, verifying (
7
). Following
(
Duval & Peyré 2015
)
, we show that for large sample sizes and with high probability,
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}
is sparse and has exactly 1 particle in each near region. We stress that in the following,
0<\tau\leq u_{\min}
is fixed (does not decrease as
n\to\infty
).

Non Degenerate Source Condition

We present here the key tool of our analysis, based again on non-degenerate dual certificates (Definition
3.2
), with additional properties. We show the existence of a global non-degenerate certificate
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
verifying
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>-1
. It is said to satisfy the Non Degenerate Source Condition (
NDSC
).

Lemma 6.1

(Non-degeneration of
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
)
.

Let
s\geq 2
and assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}
. There exist
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>0
depending only on
d
, and
\Delta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>0
depending only on
u_{\min},u_{\max},d,s,\tau
such that if
\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j=1}^{s}
satisfies
\min_{x_{i}^{0}\neq x_{j}^{0}}\mathpzc{d}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\geq\Delta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
, then there exists a
\left(\varepsilon_{0,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}},\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}},r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right)
-non-degenerate certificate of the form (
18
) (of parameters only depending on
d
), that we denote by
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
, verifying

|\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}|\leq 1\,,\quad|\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x)|=1\iff x\in\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j}\,,\quad\nabla^{2}\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})\prec 0\quad\forall\,j=1,\ldots,s\,.

(NDSC)

It is said to satisfy the Non Degenerate Source Condition (NDSC).

Under the same assumptions, for all
j=1,\ldots,s
there exists a certificate
\eta_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
for the jth near region of the form (
18
), of parameters
\left(\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}},\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}},r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right)
only depending on
d
.

Moreover,
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Psi^{*}p_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
and
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Psi^{*}p_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
where
\left\lVert p_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{2s}
and
\left\lVert p_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{2}
.

The proof is in Appendix
K.1
. We construct
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
in the same way as in Theorem
5.2
. The main difference with Theorem
5.1
is the condition
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>-1
, which is verified under a stronger separation condition on
\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j=1}^{s}
.

The certificate
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
from Lemma
6.1
is—as the global dual certificate constructed in Theorem
5.1
—the vanishing derivative pre-certificate of
(
Duval & Peyré 2015
, Section 4)
. We can then show (see Lemma
K.2
in Appendix) that
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
corresponds to the minimal norm certificate
(
Duval & Peyré 2015
, Proposition 7)
: we have
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Psi^{*}p_{0,0}
where

p_{0,0}\coloneq\underset{p\in\mathbb{L}}{\arg\min}\left\{\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\;:\;\Psi^{*}p\in\partial\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\right\}\,,

(
p_{0,0}
)

with
\partial\left\lVert\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
denoting the subdifferential of the TV-norm.
This certificate allows us to control the sparsity index of
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}
for large sample sizes, leading to the following theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
, there exists
\kappa_{0}>0
and
\gamma_{0}>0
(depending on
\mathcal{X}
,
\tau
and
\mu^{0}
) such that for all
\kappa\leq\kappa_{0}
and if
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
, then
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}
is
s
-sparse and has exactly 1 particle in each
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
.

The proof can be found in Appendix
K.2
.

This result should be seen in conjunction with Lemma
3.1
. The latter indicates that the assumption
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \gamma_{0}\kappa_{0}
is verified with high probability for
n
large enough.
We detail this in the following corollary.

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
c_{\kappa}>0
. Assume that the conditions given in Lemma
6.1
hold.
Let
n\geq\frac{c_{\kappa}^{2}}{\kappa_{0}^{2}(2\pi)^{d/2}\tau^{d}}
and
\kappa=\frac{c_{\kappa}}{(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}
. With probability greater than
1-C_{\Gamma}e^{-\left(\frac{\gamma_{0}c_{\kappa}}{C_{\Gamma}}\right)^{2}}
,

\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\omega_{j}^{\star}\delta_{x_{j}^{\star}}\quad\text{with}\quad\omega_{j}^{\star}>0\,,\quad x_{j}^{\star}\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})\quad\forall\,j=1,\ldots,s\,.

Moreover,

|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{\star}|\leq c_{0}\frac{c_{\kappa}}{\sqrt{n}}

(23)

where
c_{0}>0
depends on
\mathcal{X}
,
\tau
and
\mu^{0}
.
In addition, if
c_{\kappa}
is chosen as
c_{\kappa,n}=\underset{n\to+\infty}{o}(\sqrt{n})
, there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
depending on
(c_{\kappa,n})_{n},\mathcal{X},\tau,\mu^{0}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
,

\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq\tilde{c}_{0}\frac{c_{\kappa,n}}{\sqrt{n}}

with probability at least
1-C_{\Gamma}e^{-\left(\frac{\gamma_{0}c_{\kappa,n}}{C_{\Gamma}}\right)^{2}}
, where
\tilde{c}_{0}
depends on
\mathcal{X}
,
\tau
and
\mu^{0}
.

The proof is given in Appendix
K.3
. The result is established for a generic value of
c_{\kappa}
, but different specific choices can be considered. For instance, choosing
c_{\kappa,n}=\alpha\sqrt{\ln n}
with
\alpha>0
, there exists
n_{0,\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}
depending on
\mathcal{X},\tau,\mu^{0},\alpha
such that for all
n\geq n_{0,\alpha}
and with probability at least
1-C_{\Gamma}n^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}^{2}\alpha^{2}}{C_{\Gamma}^{2}}}
,
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\omega_{j}^{\star}\delta_{x_{j}^{\star}}
where for all
j=1,\ldots,s
,

|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{\star}|\leq c_{0}\alpha\frac{\sqrt{\ln n}}{\sqrt{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq\tilde{c}_{0}\alpha\frac{\sqrt{\ln n}}{\sqrt{n}}\,.

(24)

Compared to Theorem
3.1
, Inequality (
24
) yields more classical results in parametric estimation. We indeed obtain parametric rates of convergence (up to a logarithmic factor) for the estimation of both weight and location parameters. Moreover, the estimator has a sparsity index exactly matching that of the target measure
\mu^{0}
. These bounds hold under slightly more restrictive conditions and for sufficiently large sample sizes. We also stress that these results are obtained under a specific tuning of the parameters
\kappa
and
\tau
, which differs from that used in the previous sections.

7
Discussion and open problems

In this paper, we have described some theoretical properties of the BLASSO in the framework of Gaussian mixtures. In particular, we have considered the case where each underlying component has an unknown diagonal covariance. At this stage, further improvements and extensions are still possible and are discussed below.

Algorithmic considerations

Recall that Theorems
3.1
and
4.1
apply with any
\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}
that verifies
J_{W}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\leq J_{W}(\mu_{\omega}^{0})
: our estimator is not necessarily the solution of (

P
κ

). So we can restrict (

P
κ

) to any subset of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})^{+}
containing
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
.

This remark is interesting from an algorithmic point of view. We do not know how to solve (

P
κ

) numerically on the entire measure space
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})^{+}
, due to the absence of a parametrization of this space.
In
Hardy 2023
, a version of the BLASSO over the space of
K
-sparse measures (discrete measures with less than
K
particles) is considered.
This problem, although not convex, is closer to a realistic algorithmic framework, as we can parametrize the space of
K
-sparse measures. If
K\geq s
,
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
belongs to this space.

Several algorithms have been proposed to solve the BLASSO on sparse measures, such as the sliding Frank-Wolfe algorithm
(
Denoyelle et al. 2019
)
or the Conic Particle Gradient Descent (CPGD, see
(
Chizat 2022
)
). Adapting the latter to our framework could form the core of a future work.

Non-diagonal covariance matrices

In this contribution, we are only dealing with the case where the covariances of the mixture are diagonal.
Note that we can easily extend our model and results to the case where all covariances share the same (known) orientation,
i.e
.
we can diagonalize them in the same basis. However, we are not yet able to process general covariances with varying or unknown orientations. Indeed, the Fisher-Rao metric seems impractical to work with, mainly because it might be tricky to prove the compatibility between the associated distance and the semi-distance, and obtain the desired controls on the kernel. A possible outcome could be to consider an alternative metric.

Minimal separation condition

Our results are established under a separation condition for the mixture components. In
(
De et al. 2021
)
, the separation between the particles can go to zero as
n
grows, because a translation-invariant kernel is used. Such kernels have been investigated in
(
De et al. 2025
)
and this analysis enables the construction of certificates based on a “pivot” kernel, whose decay can increase when dealing with closer particles. This is not the case here: our kernel is not practical to build a pivot, and its decay does not allow us to consider
\Delta\to 0
(for
(t,u)\neq(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)
,
e^{-\frac{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}}{2(u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+\tau^{2})}}
cannot be as close to 0 as wanted by changing
\tau
). Moreover, the use of the semi-distance
\mathpzc{d}
imposes a larger minimal separation.

Statistical learning of Gaussian Mixtures

Gaussian mixtures have been at the core of several investigations and it is not possible to provide a complete state of the art in a single paragraph. Different tasks can be considered, ranging from clustering
(
Chen & Zhang 2024
)
, testing
(
Donoho & Jin 2004
)
or estimating the component parameters. For the latter, different settings and related assumptions have been considered. Estimating the mixture parameters is an hard task
(
Anandkumar et al. 2012
;
Doss et al. 2023
)
, even in the case where the component covariances are known. Our approach, based on convex optimization on the space of measures, leads to specific bounds related to the mass of the estimator on far and near regions. A complete comparison with alternative approaches may require additional investigations that are outside the scope of this paper.

Technical side notes

We have used the semi-distance
\mathpzc{d}
to define the near and far regions, and the Fisher-Rao metric to control the certificates with Taylor expansions. These are somewhat arbitrary or improvable choices. The semi-distance
\mathpzc{d}
seems appropriate to control the kernel, because it measures precisely how 2 points are spaced for the kernel. But the downside is that it does not satisfy the triangle inequality. As a consequence, we need to take
\Delta_{\tau}
much larger than
\Delta
. The Fisher-Rao metric allows us to retrieve quite easily global bounds for the kernel (
i.e
.
evaluations of the bounds
B_{ij}
, see item 1 of Definition
5.2
), but we could have used the Euclidean metric—although it leads to controls that depend on bounds on the variance.

Summary of the main results

The target measure is
\mu^{0}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}a_{j}^{0}\delta_{(t_{j}^{0},u_{j}^{0})}\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
.
We assume in the following that
\mathcal{X}
is a compact set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}
, and that
n\geq 2
,
s\geq 2
,
0<\tau\leq u_{\min}
.
The results displayed in the following table require more assumptions, that we explicit here. Keep in mind that
\mathpzc{d}
depends on
\tau
.

\min_{i\neq j}\mathpzc{d}(x_{i}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\geq\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{u_{\max}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{0.3025^{2}}{d}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})}}{u_{\min}}\left(\Delta+\frac{0.3025}{\sqrt{d}}\right)\>,\>2\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\Delta\right\}+\sqrt{d\ln\left(\frac{u_{\max}^{2}}{u_{\min}^{2}}\right)}

Gaussian Mixture Model with unknown diagonal covariances via continuous sparse regularization

Appendix

Appendix A
Functional framework

The BLASSO operates on the space of Radon measures. In this section, we provide definitions of the operators on measures used throughout the paper.

Let
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
In the following,
\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(A)
is the set of bounded functions from
A
to
\mathbb{R}
;
L^{1}(A)
(resp.
L^{2}(A)
) the set of functions whose absolute value (resp. square) have a finite integral. The notions of convolution and Fourier transform can be extended to measures.

Definition A.1

(Convolution
g*\mu
)
.

Let
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
. The convolution between
g\in\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(A)
and
\mu\in\mathcal{M}(A)
is defined by

g*\mu\coloneq\int_{A}g(\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}-x)\text{d}\mu(x)\,.

Definition A.2

(Fourier transform over
L^{1}(A)
and
\mathcal{M}(A)
)
.

Let
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
. We use the Fourier transform defined for
g\in L^{1}(A)
by

\mathcal{F}\left[g\right](\xi)\coloneq\int_{A}e^{-i\left\langle\xi,x\right\rangle}g(x)\,\text{d}x\,.

We also use its extension to
\mathcal{M}(A)
:

\mathcal{F}\left[\mu\right](\xi)\coloneq\int_{A}e^{-i\left\langle\xi,x\right\rangle}\,\text{d}\mu(x)\quad\forall\,\mu\in\mathcal{M}(A)\,.

The standard properties of convolution and Fourier transform apply, such as
\mathcal{F}\left[g*\mu\right]=\mathcal{F}\left[g\right]\mathcal{F}\left[\mu\right]
for all
g\in L^{1}(A)\cap\mathcal{L}^{\infty}(A)
and all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}(A)
(see, for example,
(
Rudin 1991
, Part 2)
).

Appendix B
Existence of a solution to the BLASSO

Proposition B.1
.

The problem (

P
κ

) has a solution.

Proof.

J_{W}
is lower semi-continuous on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})^{+}
for the weak* convergence:

The TV norm is lower semi-continuous for the weak* convergence. It then suffices to show that
\mu\mapsto L\circ\Phi\frac{\mu}{W}
is weak* to weak continuous.
Let
\mu_{j}\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup}\mu
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
. We want to show that for
f\in\mathbb{L}
,

\left\langle L\circ\Phi\frac{\mu_{j}}{W},f\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\rightarrow\penalty\ \left\langle L\circ\penalty\ \Phi\frac{\mu}{W},f\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,.

Using
(
Christmann & Steinwart 2008
, Lemma 4.29)
, as

K_{\rm norm}:(x,x^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d})^{2}\mapsto\left\langle L\circ\Phi\frac{\delta_{x}}{W},L\circ\Phi\frac{\delta_{x^{\prime}}}{W}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}

is continuous (see (
19
)), we have

\left(x\mapsto L\circ\Phi\frac{\delta_{x}}{W}\right)\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{L})\,.

Hence

\left(x\mapsto\left\langle L\circ\Phi\frac{\delta_{x}}{W},f\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\right)\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})\,.

As
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})^{*}
, it comes

\int\left\langle L\circ\Phi\frac{\delta_{x}}{W},f\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,\text{d}\mu_{j}(x)\rightarrow\int\left\langle L\circ\Phi\frac{\delta_{x}}{W},f\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,\text{d}\mu(x)\,,

which concludes this part of the proof.

Conclusion:
The result follows noticing that
J_{W}(\mu)\underset{\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}\infty
: we can restrict the problem to a closed ball of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})^{+}
. By the Banach–Alaoglu theorem, this ball is weakly* compact (a ball of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
is weakly* compact, and
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})^{+}
is a weakly* closed subspace of
M(\mathcal{X})
). We deduce the existence of a minimizer of
J_{W}
on
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})^{+}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
3.1

Expected value of
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}
:

Note that, according to the definition of
\Gamma_{n}
,

\Gamma_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(L\circ\delta_{X_{i}}-L\circ f^{0}\right)\,.

For all
h\in\mathbb{L}
, for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle L\circ\delta_{X_{i}},h\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\right]=\left\langle L\circ f^{0},h\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}
. This entails that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\Gamma_{n},h\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\right]=0
.
Defining

Z_{i}\coloneq L\circ\delta_{X_{i}}-L\circ f^{0}

(where we recall that
f^{0}=\Phi\mu^{0}
), observe that
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
are i.i.d. Since for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,

\left\lVert Z_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda|\mathcal{F}\left[\delta_{X_{i}}-f^{0}\right]|^{2}\leq\frac{4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda}{(2\pi)^{d}}\,,

we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert Z_{i}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle Z_{i},Z_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda}{(2\pi)^{d}n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\left\langle Z_{i},Z_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,.

We deduce that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{4\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Lambda}{(2\pi)^{d}n}
using that
\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}]=0
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.

Control in probability:
The control in probability of
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}
comes from
(
De et al. 2021a
, Lemma 3)
, and stems from results on U-processes (see
(
Arcones & Giné 1993
, Proposition 2.3)
). In particular, we have

\forall\rho>0\,,\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}>\rho\frac{C_{\Gamma}^{2}}{n\tau^{d}(2\pi)^{d/2}}\right)\leq C_{\Gamma}e^{-\rho}\,,

(25)

for some positive constant
C_{\Gamma}>0
.

Expected value of
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}
:
Using (
25
), it comes that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}>x\right)\,\text{d}x\,,

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}>\sqrt{x}\right)\,\text{d}x\,,

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}C_{\Gamma}e^{-\sqrt{x}\frac{n\tau^{d}(2\pi)^{d/2}}{C_{\Gamma}^{2}}}\,\text{d}x\,,

so

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}\right]\leq C_{\Gamma}\frac{C_{\Gamma}^{4}}{n^{2}\tau^{2d}(2\pi)^{2d}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\sqrt{x}}\,\text{d}x=\frac{2C_{\Gamma}^{5}}{n^{2}\tau^{2d}(2\pi)^{2d}}\,.

This proves the desired result.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
3.1

The following proof is standard when dealing with the BLASSO procedure. Its main arguments can be found in
(
De et al. 2021
)
; we adapt their proof to our setting. It is based on bounds on the Bregman divergence introduced in (
13
). With
W
defined by (
6
), we denote in the following
\hat{\mu}_{n}=\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}
. We also recall from (
9
) that
\mu^{0}=\frac{\mu_{\omega}^{0}}{W}
.

Upper bound on the Bregman divergence:
Since
J_{W}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\leq J_{W}(\mu_{\omega}^{0})
, we get

\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+2\kappa\left\lVert\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+2\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}

(26)

which can be rewritten as

\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+2\kappa D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)+2\kappa\int_{\mathcal{X}}\eta\,\text{d}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}-\mu_{\omega}^{0})\leq\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\,,

(27)

for any dual certificate
\eta
satisfying the requirements of Assumption
1
. As
\eta=\Psi^{*}p
, recalling (
10
) we have

\int_{\mathcal{X}}\eta\,\text{d}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}-\mu_{\omega}^{0})=\left\langle p,L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,.

So using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (
27
) leads to

\left\lVert L\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+2\kappa D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)-2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\left\lVert L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\,.

The triangle inequality

\left\lVert L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+\left\lVert L\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}

then leads to

\left(\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}-\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right)^{2}+2\kappa D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\leq\left(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right)^{2}\,.

As the Bregman divergence is positive, we deduce from the previous inequality that

D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\leq\frac{\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}}{2\kappa}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\,.

(28)

The bound in expected value on the Bregman divergence follows by applying Lemma
3.1
and using
\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{c_{p}s}
. We have

\mathbb{E}\left[D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\right]\leq\frac{\rho_{n}^{2}}{2\kappa}+\kappa\frac{c_{p}}{2}s+\rho_{n}\sqrt{c_{p}s}\,.

(29)

Taking
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}}}
gives

\mathbb{E}\left[D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\right]\leq\frac{\sqrt{c_{p}}}{2}\rho_{n}(1+\sqrt{s})^{2}\,.

(30)

Lower bound on the Bregman divergence:
We use the controls of the non-degenerate certificate
\eta
on the near and far regions. Since
\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}
is nonnegative, according to Definition
3.2
we have

D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)=\int(1-\eta)\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}^{far}(r))+\varepsilon_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(x)\,.

(31)

Combining (
30
) and (
31
), we deduce the bound for the far region (item 1 of Theorem
3.1
).
To control the mass of the estimator on the jth near region, we make use of the local non-degenerate certificate
\eta_{j}
(Definition
3.3
).
We have, for all
j=1,\ldots,s
,

\displaystyle|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r))|

\displaystyle=\left|\omega_{j}^{0}-\int\eta_{j}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+\int\eta_{j}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}-\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)}\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\right|\,,

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\eta_{j}\,\text{d}(\mu_{\omega}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\right|+\int_{\mathcal{X}^{near}(r)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)}|\eta_{j}|\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)}|1-\eta_{j}|\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathcal{X}^{far}(r)}|\eta_{j}|\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\,,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq|\left\langle p_{j},L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}|+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\sum_{l=1}^{s}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{l}^{near}(r)}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{l}^{0})^{2}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(x)\\
&\quad+(1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}^{far}(r))\,,\end{split}

(32)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}(2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})+\max\left\{\frac{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}{\varepsilon_{2}}\right\}D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\,,

where for the last inequality, we have used (
31
), and

|\left\langle p_{j},L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}|\leq\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\left(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right)\leq\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\left(2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right)

together with (
28
).
As
\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{c_{p}}
,
\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{c_{p}s}
and
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}}}
, we finally have

|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r))|\leq 2\sqrt{c_{p}}(\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+\rho_{n}\sqrt{s})+\max\left\{\frac{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}{\varepsilon_{2}}\right\}D_{\eta}\left(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\,.

The result in expected value follows from
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right]\leq\rho_{n}
(Lemma
3.1
used with Jensen’s inequality) and (
30
).

For the stability of the mass (item 3), we use a similar but simpler reasoning. As
\eta\leq 1
and
\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}
is nonnegative, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}-\left\lVert\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}

\displaystyle=\int\eta\,(\text{d}\mu_{\omega}^{0}-\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})+\int(\eta-1)\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\int\eta\,(\text{d}\mu_{\omega}^{0}-\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\,,

\displaystyle=\left\langle p,L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}(2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})\,.

Using (
26
), we also have
\left\lVert\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}+\frac{1}{2\kappa}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}
. We can conclude by taking the expectation in these inequalities and using Lemma
3.1
.

With an
s
-dependent choice of regularization:
Choosing
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}s}}
, (
29
) gives
\mathbb{E}\left[D_{\eta}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right]\leq 2\rho_{n}\sqrt{c_{p}s}
. It comes that

\mathbb{E}\left[|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r))|\right]\leq 2\sqrt{c_{p}}\rho_{n}(1+\sqrt{s})+\max\left\{\frac{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}{\varepsilon_{2}}\right\}2\rho_{n}\sqrt{c_{p}s}\,.

(33)

Appendix E
Basic inequalities

The following lemma gives inequalities useful when dealing with the semi-distance
\mathpzc{d}
. We will use them in various proofs.

Lemma E.1

(Basic inequalities)
.

Let
a,b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
,
c\geq 1
. Then

\frac{a^{2}+b^{2}}{2ab}\leq c\iff a\in\left[b(c-\sqrt{c^{2}-1}),b(c+\sqrt{c^{2}-1})\right]

(34)

and

\frac{a^{2}+b^{2}}{2ab}\leq c\implies\frac{|a^{2}-b^{2}|}{a^{2}+b^{2}}\leq\sqrt{c^{2}-1}

(35)

along with

\frac{a^{2}+b^{2}}{2ab}\leq c\implies\frac{2a^{2}}{a^{2}+b^{2}}\leq c+\sqrt{c^{2}-1}\,.

(36)

Proof.

Let
a,b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
and
c\geq 1
.

Proof of (
34
):
We have

\frac{a^{2}+b^{2}}{2ab}\leq c\iff a^{2}+b^{2}-2cab\leq 0\,.

As
4c^{2}b^{2}-4b^{2}=4b^{2}(c^{2}-1)\geq 0
, the roots of this polynomial in
a
are

\frac{2cb\pm 2b\sqrt{c^{2}-1}}{2}=b(c\pm\sqrt{c^{2}-1})

from which we deduce (
34
).

Proof of (
35
):

Using (
34
) and
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{|a^{2}-b^{2}|}{a^{2}+b^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|a-b||a+b|}{2ab}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\min\{a,b\}(1+c+\sqrt{c^{2}-1})\max\{a,b\}(1-(c-\sqrt{c^{2}-1}))}{2ab}\,,

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+c+\sqrt{c^{2}-1})(1+\sqrt{c^{2}-1}-c)}{2}\,,

\displaystyle=\sqrt{c^{2}-1}\,.

Proof of (
36
):

Using again (
34
) with
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2a^{2}}{a^{2}+b^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2ab(c+\sqrt{c^{2}-1})}{2ab}\,,

\displaystyle\leq c+\sqrt{c^{2}-1}\,.

∎

Appendix F
Proofs related to the control of the estimator on the effective near regions

F.1
Proof of Proposition
3.1

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
3.1
in Section
D
. Let
j\in\{1,\dots,s\}
and
\eta_{j}
a corresponding local non-degenerate dual certificate satisfying the requirements of Assumption
1
. According to Definition
3.3
, for all
x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)
, we have
|\eta_{j}|\leq 1+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2}
. We deduce that

\displaystyle|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))|

\displaystyle=\left|\omega_{j}^{0}-\int\eta_{j}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+\int\eta_{j}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}-\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\right|\,,

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\eta_{j}\,\text{d}(\mu_{\omega}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\right|+\int_{\mathcal{X}^{near}(r)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)}|\eta_{j}|\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}|\eta_{j}|\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}|1-\eta_{j}|\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+\int_{\mathcal{X}^{far}(r)}|\eta_{j}|\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}(2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\sum_{l\neq j}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{l}^{near}(r)}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{l}^{0})^{2}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+(1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0})\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}^{far}(r))

\displaystyle\quad+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2}\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}+\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}(1+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2})\,\text{d}\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\,.

From (
15
) we get
1\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}}{r_{e}^{2}}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{j}^{0},x)^{2}
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})
, so
1+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{j}^{0},x)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}}{r_{e}^{2}}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\right)\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{j}^{0},x)^{2}
.
Using again (
31
), we deduce that

|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))|\leq\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}(2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})+\max\left\{\frac{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}}{r_{e}^{2}}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\right)\right\}D_{\eta}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0})\,.

(37)

We can conclude the proof using the controls on
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right]
,
\left\lVert p_{j}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}
,
\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[D_{\eta}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right]
stemming from our assumptions along with Lemma
3.1
and (
30
). Choosing
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}}}
,

|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))|\leq 2\sqrt{c_{p}}\rho_{n}(1+\sqrt{s})+\max\left\{\frac{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}}{r_{e}^{2}}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\right)\right\}\frac{\sqrt{c_{p}}}{2}\rho_{n}(1+\sqrt{s})^{2}\,.

With an
s
-dependent choice of regularization:
Choosing
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}s}}
, using (
37
) and the control of
\mathbb{E}\left[D_{\eta}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega},\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right]
stemming from (
29
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{c_{p}}\rho_{n}+\max\left\{\frac{1-\tilde{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\varepsilon_{0}},\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3}}{r_{e}^{2}}+\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}\right)\right\}2\rho_{n}\sqrt{c_{p}s}\,,

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{s}}{\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}r_{e}^{2}}

(38)

keeping only the dependence on
s,\tau,r_{e},n
.

F.2
Proof of Corollary
3.1

Let
0<r_{e}\leq r
.
We first prove that

\left|\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))-a_{j}^{0}\right|\leq(1+H(r)r_{e})W(x_{j}^{0})^{-1}\left|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))\right|+a_{j}^{0}H(r)r_{e}

(39)

where

H(r)=\frac{(e^{r^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r^{2}}-1})^{d/2}-1}{r}\,.

(40)

We use the triangle inequality

\left|a_{j}^{0}-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))\right|\leq\underbrace{\left|a_{j}^{0}-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}\right|}_{\eqcolon A}+\underbrace{\left|\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))\right|}_{\eqcolon B}\,.

Control of
B
:
We recall the definitions of effective near regions (
14
) and of
W
(see (
6
)). We have

\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\frac{W(x_{j}^{0})}{W(x)}\,\text{d}\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W(x_{j}^{0})}(x)

so

\left|\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}\right|\leq\max\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\frac{W(x_{j}^{0})}{W(x)}-1\,,\,1-\inf_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\frac{W(x_{j}^{0})}{W(x)}\right\}\times\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}\,.

For
x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})
, as
\prod_{k=1}^{d}\frac{(u_{j,k}^{0})^{2}+u_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2}}{\sqrt{2(u_{j,k}^{0})^{2}+\tau^{2}}\sqrt{2u_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2}}}\leq e^{r_{e}^{2}}
and as each term of this product is greater than
1
, we have
\frac{(u_{j,k}^{0})^{2}+u_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2}}{\sqrt{2(u_{j,k}^{0})^{2}+\tau^{2}}\sqrt{2u_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2}}}\leq e^{r_{e}^{2}}
for all
k=1,\ldots,d
. Using (
34
), this implies

\sqrt{u_{k}^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\in\left[\sqrt{(u_{j,k}^{0})^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}(e^{r_{e}^{2}}-\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1}),\sqrt{(u_{j,k}^{0})^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})\right]\;\forall\,k=1,\ldots,d\,,

from which we deduce that

\displaystyle\max\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\frac{W(x_{j}^{0})}{W(x)}-1\,,\,1-\inf_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e})}\frac{W(x_{j}^{0})}{W(x)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}-1\,,\,1-(e^{r_{e}^{2}}-\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}\right\}\,,

\displaystyle=(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}-1

where we used that
(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}\geq 1
to establish that

1-(e^{r_{e}^{2}}-\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}=1-(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{-d/2}\leq(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}-1\,.

Hence

\left|\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}\right|\leq\left((e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}-1\right)\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}\,.

(41)

Control of A and proof of (
39
):

As

\left|a_{j}^{0}-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))}{W(x_{j}^{0})}\right|=W(x_{j}^{0})^{-1}\left|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))\right|\,,

from (
41
) we get

\left|\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))-a_{j}^{0}\right|\leq(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}W(x_{j}^{0})^{-1}|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))|+a_{j}^{0}\left((e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}-1\right)\,.

We conclude the proof of (
39
) by noticing that
h:r_{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\mapsto(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}
is convex (for all
d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
), hence for
r_{e}\leq r
we have

(e^{r_{e}^{2}}+\sqrt{e^{2r_{e}^{2}}-1})^{d/2}\leq h(0)+\frac{h(r)-h(0)}{r}r_{e}=1+\frac{h(r)-1}{r}r_{e}\,.

Conclusion:

Taking
r_{e}=n^{-\alpha}
with
\alpha>0
, (
39
) gives

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|a_{j}^{0}-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(n^{-\alpha}))\right|\right]\leq W(x_{j}^{0})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(n^{-\alpha}))\right|\right]\left(1+H(r)n^{-\alpha}\right)+a_{j}^{0}H(r)n^{-\alpha}

where
H(r)
is defined by (
40
).
Proposition
3.1
gives
\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\omega_{j}^{0}-\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(n^{-\alpha}))\right|\right]\lesssim\frac{s}{\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}r_{e}^{2}}=\frac{s}{\tau^{d/2}n^{1/2-2\alpha}}
. We choose
r_{e}=n^{-1/6}
to balance the terms.

With
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}s}}
:
Choosing
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}s}}
, using (
38
) we get for
n^{-1/6}\leq r

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|a_{j}^{0}-\frac{\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}}{W}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(n^{-1/6}))\right|\right]\lesssim\left(W(x_{j}^{0})^{-1}\sqrt{s}\tau^{-d/2}+a_{j}^{0}\right)n^{-1/6}\,.

(42)

Appendix G
Proofs related to guarantees on the prediction

We will use the following lemma to go from controls of
\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}
to controls on
\left\lVert\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}
.

Lemma G.1

(Control of the high frequencies)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
. Let
\tau>0
. We work with
\Lambda(\xi)=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\tau^{2}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}
. Let
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\penalty\ \mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
. Then

\left\lVert\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq e\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\mu_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})\frac{\tau^{d}d^{d/2}}{2^{d/2}u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-2\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}}\,.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{T}>0
. First write

\left\lVert\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right]|^{2}+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right]|^{2}\,.

Then, remark that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right]|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}\frac{\Lambda}{\Lambda}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right]|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{d\tau^{2}}{2\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}\Lambda|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right]|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{d\tau^{2}}{2\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\,.

Concerning the high frequencies of
\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})
, recall that
u_{1},\ldots,u_{d}\geq u_{\min}
for all
((t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}),(u_{1},\ldots,u_{d}))\in\mathcal{X}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right]|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\mu_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}e^{-u_{\min}^{2}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}\,\text{d}\xi\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\mu_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})\left(\frac{\mathrm{T}}{u_{\min}^{2}}e^{-\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\right)^{d}

using

\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]}e^{-u_{\min}^{2}z^{2}}\,\text{d}z=2\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\int_{\left[1,+\infty\right)}e^{-u_{\min}^{2}\frac{z^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\,\text{d}z\leq 2\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\int_{\left[1,+\infty\right)}ze^{-u_{\min}^{2}\frac{z^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\,\text{d}z=\frac{\mathrm{T}}{u_{\min}^{2}}e^{-\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\,.

(43)

So

\left\lVert\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leq e^{\frac{d\tau^{2}}{2\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\mu_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})\left(\frac{\mathrm{T}}{u_{\min}^{2}}e^{-\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}\right)^{d}\,.

Taking
\mathrm{T}=\frac{\tau\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{2}}
,
we get

\left\lVert\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq e\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\mu_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})\frac{\tau^{d}d^{d/2}}{2^{d/2}u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-2\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}}\,.

∎

G.1
Proof of Proposition
4.1

We do not make any assumption on the existence of dual certificates in this proof.
From
J_{W}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\leq J_{W}(\mu_{\omega}^{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+2\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+4\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\,.

Combining this inequality with Lemma
G.1
, we get

\left\lVert\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq e\left(4\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+4\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\right)+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\mu_{1}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{2}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})\frac{\tau^{d}d^{d/2}}{2^{d/2}u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-2\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}}\,.

Remark that for
\mu\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
we have
\left\lVert\frac{\mu}{W}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\sup_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{1}{W}\leq(2\pi)^{d/4}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{d/4}\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
, and in the same way
\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq(2\pi)^{-d/4}(2u_{\min}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{-d/4}\left\lVert\frac{\mu}{W}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
. As
J_{W}(\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega})\leq J_{W}(\mu_{\omega}^{0})
implies that
\left\lVert\hat{\mu}_{n,\omega}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\frac{1}{2\kappa}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}(\left\lVert\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}+\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left((2\pi)^{d/2}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{d/2}\left(\frac{1}{2\kappa^{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}+2\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\right)+\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2\pi)^{-d/2}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{d/2}}{\kappa^{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(2\left(\frac{2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2}}{2u_{\min}^{2}+\tau^{2}}\right)^{d/2}+1\right)\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(2\pi)^{-d/2}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{d/2}}{\kappa^{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(2\left(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\right)^{d}+1\right)\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}

(44)

using that
\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\mapsto\frac{2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2}}{2u_{\min}^{2}+\tau^{2}}
is decreasing. Hence

\left\lVert\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq e\left(4\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+4\kappa(2\pi)^{-d/4}(2u_{\min}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{-d/4}\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\right)\\
+\left(\frac{(2\pi)^{-d/2}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{d/2}}{\kappa^{2}}\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{4}+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(2\left(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\right)^{d}+1\right)\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\right)\frac{\tau^{d}d^{d/2}}{2^{d/2}u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-2\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}}\,.

With Lemma
3.1
, choosing
\tau=\frac{\sqrt{2}u_{\min}}{\sqrt{\ln n}}
and
\kappa=\rho_{n}^{2}
it comes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4e\rho_{n}^{2}\left(1+(2\pi)^{-d/4}(\sqrt{2}u_{\min})^{-d/2}\left(1+\frac{1}{\ln n}\right)^{-d/4}\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\right)

\displaystyle\;\;+\left(\tilde{C}_{\Gamma}\pi^{-d/2}\left(u_{\max}^{2}+\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\ln n}\right)^{d/2}+2(2\pi)^{-d}\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\left(2\left(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\right)^{d}+1\right)\right)\frac{d^{d/2}}{(\ln n)^{d/2}u_{\min}^{d}n}\,,

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(\ln n)^{d/2}}{n}\,.

G.2
Prediction with Kernel Density Estimation

Lemma G.2
.

With
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
, setting
\tau=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ln n}n^{\frac{1}{4+d}}}
, omitting the dependence on
d
we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{(\ln n)^{d/2}}{n^{\frac{4}{d+4}}u_{\min}^{d+4}}\,.

Proof.

Control of
\left\lVert L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}
:

As
|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi\mu^{0}\right](\xi)|^{2}\leq\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}e^{-u_{\min}^{2}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}
for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and using (
43
), it comes that for
\mathrm{T}>0
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\Lambda(\xi)-1|^{2}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi\mu^{0}\right](\xi)|^{2}\,\text{d}\xi\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi\mu^{0}\right](\xi)|^{2}\,\text{d}\xi+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\frac{d^{2}\tau^{4}}{4\mathrm{T}^{4}}\int_{\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}}|\mathcal{F}\left[\Phi\mu^{0}\right](\xi)|^{2}\,\text{d}\xi\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{T}^{d}}{u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-\frac{du_{\min}^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}+\frac{2^{d}}{\mathrm{T}^{d}}\frac{\tau^{4}d^{2}}{4\mathrm{T}^{4}}\right)\,,

where we used that for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
|\Lambda(\xi)-1|^{2}=|e^{-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}\left\lVert\xi\right\rVert_{2}^{2}}-1|\leq 1
and

|\Lambda(\xi)-1|^{2}\leq|e^{-\frac{d\tau^{2}}{2\mathrm{T}^{2}}}-1|\leq\frac{d^{2}\tau^{4}}{4\mathrm{T}^{4}}\quad\forall\,\xi\in\left[-\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}},\frac{1}{\mathrm{T}}\right]^{d}\,.

Control of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]
:

Using Lemma
3.1
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]\leq 2\rho_{n}^{2}+2\left\lVert L\circ\Phi\mu^{0}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{8}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\tau^{d}n}+\frac{2\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{T}^{d}}{u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-\frac{du_{\min}^{2}}{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}+\frac{2^{d}}{\mathrm{T}^{d}}\frac{\tau^{4}d^{2}}{4\mathrm{T}^{4}}\right)\,.

To balance these terms, we choose
\mathrm{T}=\frac{u_{\min}\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{\ln n}}
and
\tau=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\ln n}n^{\frac{1}{4+d}}}
.
It comes

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\hat{f}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{8(\ln n)^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}n^{\frac{4}{d+4}}}+\frac{2\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(\frac{d^{d/2}}{u_{\min}^{d}(\ln n)^{d/2}n}+\frac{2^{d}(\ln n)^{d/2}}{4d^{d/2}u_{\min}^{d+4}n^{\frac{4}{d+4}}}\right)\,.

∎

G.3
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Equation (
28
) gives

\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq 2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}

from which we deduce, using Lemma
3.1
and
\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{c_{p}s}
, that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\right]\leq 4(\rho_{n}+\kappa\sqrt{c_{p}s})^{2}\,.

To go from a control of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\right]
to a bound on
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]
, we use (
44
) with Lemmas
G.1
and
3.1
. We get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq e\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert L\circ\Phi(\hat{\mu}_{n}-\mu^{0})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{(2\pi)^{d}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\hat{\mu}_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\right]+\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\right)\frac{\tau^{d}d^{d/2}}{2^{d/2}u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-2\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq 4e(\rho_{n}+\kappa\sqrt{c_{p}s})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(\frac{(2\pi)^{d/2}(2u_{\max}^{2}+\tau^{2})^{d/2}\tilde{C}_{\Gamma}\rho_{n}^{4}}{\kappa^{2}}+2\bigg(2\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\bigg)^{d}+1\bigg)\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\bigg)\frac{(2\pi)^{-d}\tau^{d}d^{d/2}}{2^{d/2}u_{\min}^{2d}}e^{-2\frac{u_{\min}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq 4e(\rho_{n}+\kappa\sqrt{c_{p}s})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(4\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}^{2}}{u_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\ln n}\bigg)^{d/2}\tilde{C}_{\Gamma}\frac{(\ln n)^{d/2}\rho_{n}^{2}}{n\kappa^{2}}+2\bigg(2\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\bigg)^{d}+1\bigg)\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\bigg)\frac{(2\pi)^{-d}d^{d/2}}{u_{\min}^{d}(\ln n)^{d/2}n}\,.

With the choice
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}}}
, it comes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4e\rho_{n}^{2}(1+\sqrt{s})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(4\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}^{2}}{u_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\ln n}\bigg)^{d/2}\tilde{C}_{\Gamma}c_{p}\frac{(\ln n)^{d/2}}{n}+2\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\bigg(2\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\bigg)^{d}+1\bigg)\bigg)\frac{(2\pi)^{-d}d^{d/2}}{u_{\min}^{d}(\ln n)^{d/2}n}\,.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
With the choice
\kappa=\frac{\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{c_{p}s}}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\Phi\hat{\mu}_{n}-\Phi\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 16e\rho_{n}^{2}+\bigg(4\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}^{2}}{u_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\ln n}\bigg)^{d/2}\tilde{C}_{\Gamma}c_{p}\frac{s(\ln n)^{d/2}}{n}+2\left\lVert\mu^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}^{2}\left(2\bigg(\frac{u_{\max}}{u_{\min}}\right)^{d}+1\bigg)\bigg)\frac{(2\pi)^{-d}d^{d/2}}{u_{\min}^{d}(\ln n)^{d/2}n}\,,

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{s}{n(\ln n)^{d/2}}+1\bigg)\frac{(\ln n)^{d/2}}{n}

(45)

keeping only the dependence on
n
and
s
.

Appendix H
Properties of the Fisher-Rao metric

We present properties associated with the Fisher-Rao metric
\mathfrak{g}
, defined at point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
by
\mathfrak{g}_{x}=\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}K_{\rm norm}(x,x)
(see also (
20
)). Note that this metric depends on the smoothing parameter
\tau>0
through
K_{\rm norm}
. We recall the definition of the Riemannian norm: for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
, we define
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{x}=\sqrt{v^{T}\mathfrak{g}_{x}v}
.

H.1
Christoffel symbols

The non-zero Christoffel symbols associated with
\mathfrak{g}
are

\displaystyle\Gamma^{t_{k}}{}_{u_{k}t_{k}}=\Gamma^{t_{k}}{}_{t_{k}u_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-2u_{k}}{2u_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{u_{k}}{}_{t_{k}t_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u_{k}}\,,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{u_{k}}{}_{u_{k}u_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau^{2}-2u_{k}^{2}}{u_{k}(2u_{k}^{2}+\tau^{2})}

with
k=1,\ldots,d
(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section I.1)
).
We define

\Gamma^{t_{k}}=\begin{pmatrix}(\Gamma^{t_{k}}{}_{t_{l}t_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}&(\Gamma^{t_{k}}{}_{t_{l}u_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}\\
(\Gamma^{t_{k}}{}_{u_{l}t_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}&(\Gamma^{t_{k}}{}_{u_{l}u_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma^{u_{k}}=\begin{pmatrix}(\Gamma^{u_{k}}{}_{t_{l}t_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}&(\Gamma^{u_{k}}{}_{t_{l}u_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}\\
(\Gamma^{u_{k}}{}_{u_{l}t_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}&(\Gamma^{u_{k}}{}_{u_{l}u_{m}})_{1\leq l,m\leq d}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(46)

H.2
Geodesics and geodesic distance

The next lemmas provide the parametrization of the geodesics associated with the Fisher-Rao metric. We denote
\tilde{\gamma}
a geodesic parametrized by arc length connecting the points
x=\tilde{\gamma}(0),x^{\prime}=\tilde{\gamma}(l)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
. The parameter
l
is the geodesic distance between
x
and
x^{\prime}
, denoted by
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x^{\prime})
.
We also denote
\gamma:y\in[0,1]\mapsto\tilde{\gamma}(ly)
(it is the geodesic such that
x=\gamma(0)
,
x^{\prime}=\gamma(1)
) and
\dot{\gamma}
its derivative.

We do not use the formula of the geodesic distance
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}
in this paper, but we give it in the next lemmas for information.

Lemma H.1

(Geodesics of the Poincaré half-plane model)
.

The Poincaré half-plane is
\{x=(t,u)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\}
, on which we consider the metric defined by
\mathfrak{h}_{x}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{u^{2}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{u^{2}}\end{pmatrix}
for all
x=(t,u)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
. The associated norm is defined by
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{x}=\sqrt{v^{T}\mathfrak{h}_{x}v}
for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

The Poincaré geodesics are circular arcs whose origin is on the axis
\{u=0\}
and straight vertical lines (parallel to
\{t=0\}
).

A Poincaré geodesic parametrized by arc-length, denoted by
\tilde{h}=(\tilde{h}_{t},\tilde{h}_{u})
, is of the form

\tilde{h}:y\in[0,l]\mapsto\left(\frac{\tanh(C_{2}+y)}{C_{1}}+C_{3},\frac{1}{\cosh(C_{2}+y)|C_{1}|}\right)

(semicircle) or

\tilde{h}:y\in[0,l]\mapsto(C_{3},|C_{1}|e^{y})\quad\text{or}\quad\tilde{h}:y\in[0,l]\mapsto(C_{3},|C_{1}|e^{-y})

(straight line), where
C_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{*}
,
C_{2},C_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
,
l\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Moreover, writing
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}
the Poincaré distance,

\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}(x,x^{\prime})=\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+(u+u^{\prime})^{2}}+\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+(u-u^{\prime})^{2}}}{\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+(u+u^{\prime})^{2}}-\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+(u-u^{\prime})^{2}}}\right)\quad\forall\,x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}\,.

Proof.

The fact that the Poincaré geodesics are semicircles whose origin is on the axis
\{u=0\}
and straight lines parallel to
\{t=0\}
is well-known (see for instance
(
Stahl 1993
, Theorem 4.2.1)
).
The formula for
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}
can be found in
(
Beardon 1983
, Theorem 7.2.1)
.

We can check that the parametrizations given for the geodesics verify the geodesic equations

\begin{cases}\frac{(\dot{\tilde{h}}_{t})^{2}+(\dot{\tilde{h}}_{u})^{2}}{\tilde{h}_{u}^{2}}=1\\
\ddot{\tilde{h}}_{t}-2\frac{\dot{\tilde{h}}_{t}\dot{\tilde{h}}_{u}}{\tilde{h}_{u}}=0\\
\ddot{\tilde{h}}_{u}-\frac{(\dot{\tilde{h}}_{u})^{2}}{\tilde{h}_{u}}+\frac{(\dot{\tilde{h}}_{t})^{2}}{\tilde{h}_{u}}=0\end{cases}\,,

as done in
(
Giard 2025
, Section I.2)
.
We found all the geodesics, because all the portions of Poincaré semicircles and straight lines can be obtained with appropriate choices of
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},l
.
∎

Lemma H.2

(Geodesics for
d=1
)
.

Let
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)
.

•

If
t=t^{\prime}
, the geodesic is of the form

\tilde{\gamma}(y)=\left(c_{3},\sqrt{\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}e^{\sqrt{8}y}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)\quad\forall\,y\in[0,l]\quad\text{or}\quad\tilde{\gamma}(y)=\left(c_{3},\sqrt{\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}e^{-\sqrt{8}y}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)\quad\forall\,y\in[0,l]

(47)

where
c_{1},c_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
. It is a portion of a straight line parallel to
\{t=0\}
.

•

If
t\neq t^{\prime}
, the geodesic is of the form

\tilde{\gamma}(y)=\left(c_{3}+\frac{\sqrt{2}\tanh{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}{2c_{1}},\ \sqrt{-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}+\frac{1-\tanh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}{2c_{1}^{2}}}\right)\quad\forall\,y\in[0,l]

(48)

where
c_{1}\neq 0
,
c_{2},c_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
. It is a portion of a semicircle with center
(c_{3},0)
and radius

\sqrt{\frac{1}{2c_{1}^{2}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{-(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+u^{2}-u^{\prime 2}}{t^{\prime}-t}\right)^{2}+u^{2}}\,.

(49)

Moreover, the Fisher-Rao distance between
x
and
x^{\prime}
is

\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x^{\prime})=\sqrt{2}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}-\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}+\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}+\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}}{\sqrt{2}(2u^{2}+\tau^{2})^{1/4}(2u^{\prime 2}+\tau^{2})^{1/4}}\right)\,.

Proof.

Link with the Poincaré half-plane model:

Recall that the variational formulation of a (Fisher-Rao) geodesic
\gamma=(\gamma_{t},\gamma_{u})
connecting
x,x^{\prime}
is

\inf_{\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\dot{\gamma}_{t}(y)^{2}\frac{1}{2\gamma_{u}(y)^{2}+\tau^{2}}+\dot{\gamma}_{u}(y)^{2}\frac{2\gamma_{u}(y)^{2}}{(2\gamma_{u}(y)^{2}+\tau^{2})^{2}}}\,\text{d}y\,.

We use the change of variable
h=(h_{t},h_{u})=\left(\gamma_{t},\sqrt{\gamma_{u}^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)
. Noticing that
(\dot{h}_{u})^{2}=\frac{\dot{\gamma}_{u}(y)^{2}\gamma_{u}(y)^{2}}{\gamma_{u}^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}
, it comes that the variational formulation is equivalent to the problem

\inf\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{(\dot{h}_{t}(y))^{2}\frac{1}{h_{u}(y)^{2}}+(\dot{h}_{u}(y))^{2}\frac{1}{h_{u}(y)^{2}}}\,\text{d}y\>:\>h(0)=\left(t,\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right),h(1)=\left(t^{\prime},\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)\right\}\,,

and we recognize the Poincaré metric tensor (
\frac{\text{d}t^{2}+\text{d}u^{2}}{u^{2}}
) in this formulation. So
h
is the geodesic for the Poincaré half-plane metric connecting
\left(t,\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)
and
\left(t^{\prime},\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)
.

Geodesic distance:

In particular, using the formula for
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}
in Lemma
H.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{h}}\left(\left(t,\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right),\left(t^{\prime},\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)\right)\,,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}+\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}+\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}-\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}}{\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}+\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}-\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}-\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}-\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}+\sqrt{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+\left(\sqrt{u^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}+\sqrt{u^{\prime 2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)^{2}}}{\sqrt{2}(2u^{2}+\tau^{2})^{1/4}(2u^{\prime 2}+\tau^{2})^{1/4}}\right)\,.

Parametrization by arc-length:
We saw that
\left(\gamma_{t},\sqrt{\gamma_{u}^{2}+\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)
is a geodesic for the Poincaré half-plane model. Lemma
H.1
gives its parametrization by arc-length,
\tilde{h}
.
For the
\mathfrak{h}
-norm,
\left\lVert\dot{\tilde{h}}_{t}(y),\dot{\tilde{h}}_{u}(y)\right\rVert_{\tilde{h}(y)}=1
.
We deduce that for the
\mathfrak{g}
-norm, writing
g(y)=\left(\tilde{h}_{t}(y),\sqrt{\tilde{h}_{u}(y)^{2}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}}\right)
, we have
\left\lVert\dot{g}_{t}(y),\dot{g}_{u}(y)\right\rVert_{g(y)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
. So defining
\tilde{\gamma}(y)=g(\sqrt{2}y)
, it comes that
\left\lVert\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}_{t}(y),\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}_{u}(y)\right\rVert_{\gamma(y)}=1
for the
\mathfrak{g}
-norm:
\tilde{\gamma}
is a geodesic parametrized by arc-length. The geodesics we provide are of this form.

Radius of the semicircle connecting
x
and
x^{\prime}
:
The parametrization of the semicircle gives

(\tilde{\gamma}_{t}-c_{3})^{2}+\tilde{\gamma}_{u}^{2}=\frac{1}{2c_{1}^{2}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}

which is the square of the radius of the semicircle. We also have

\frac{1}{2c_{1}^{2}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}=(t-c_{3})^{2}+u^{2}=(t^{\prime}-c_{3})^{2}+u^{\prime 2}\,,

from which we deduce that
c_{3}=\frac{t^{\prime 2}+u^{\prime 2}-(t^{2}+u^{2})}{2(t^{\prime}-t)}
along with
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2c_{1}^{2}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{-(t-t^{\prime})^{2}+u^{2}-u^{\prime 2}}{t^{\prime}-t}\right)^{2}+u^{2}
.
∎

We can extend this result to higher dimensions. By abuse of notation,
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})
will refer to the Fisher-Rao distance in dimension 1 between
x_{k}
and
x_{k}^{\prime}
, for all
k\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
. The notation
\mathfrak{g}_{x_{k}}
follows the same principle.

Lemma H.3

(Geodesics in dimension
d\geq 1
)
.

Let
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
.
For
k=1,\ldots,d
, we denote
\bar{\gamma}_{k}
the geodesic in dimension 1, parametrized by arc length connecting
x_{k}=(t_{k},u_{k})
and
x_{k}^{\prime}=(t_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime})
.
The geodesic connecting
x
and
x^{\prime}
is of the form

\tilde{\gamma}=(\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{1}},\ldots,\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{d}},\tilde{\gamma}_{u_{1}},\ldots,\tilde{\gamma}_{u_{d}})\quad\text{where}\quad(\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{k}}(y),\tilde{\gamma}_{u_{k}}(y))=\bar{\gamma}_{k}(\sqrt{g_{k}}y)\quad\text{with}\quad g_{k}\geq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}=1\,.

Moreover,

\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x^{\prime})=\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})^{2}}\,.

Proof.

For all
k\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
, we denote
\mathfrak{d}_{k}=\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})^{2}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}(y)=\bar{\gamma}_{k}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{d}_{k}}}{\sqrt{\sum_{j}\mathfrak{d}_{j}}}y\right)
.
Then, for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
, we have

\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{x}^{2}=v^{T}\mathfrak{g}_{x}v=\sum_{k=1}^{d}(v_{k},v_{k+d})\mathfrak{g}_{x_{k}}(v_{k},v_{k+d})^{T},

where
(v_{k},v_{k+d})
represents the
k
-th component of
v
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
. Using this, the geodesic
\tilde{\gamma}
connecting
x
and
x^{\prime}
can be expressed as:

\tilde{\gamma}=(\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{1}},\ldots,\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{d}},\tilde{\gamma}_{u_{1}},\ldots,\tilde{\gamma}_{u_{d}}),

where each
(\tilde{\gamma}_{t_{k}},\tilde{\gamma}_{u_{k}})
corresponds to the geodesic
\tilde{\gamma}_{k}
in dimension 1 connecting
x_{k}=(t_{k},u_{k})
and
x_{k}^{\prime}=(t_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime})
. This ensures that
\tilde{\gamma}
is the geodesic connecting
x
and
x^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
.

∎

H.3
Compatibility with the semi-distance

H.3.1
Proof of Lemma
5.2

Let
r>0
and
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
. Let
\gamma
be a geodesic between
x_{0}=\gamma(0)
and
x=\gamma(1)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
for the metric
\mathfrak{g}
.

Lower bound on the variance

We write
\gamma=(\gamma_{t_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{t_{d}},\gamma_{u_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{u_{d}})
. Recall that
(\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{u_{k}})
is a portion of a straight line parallel to
\{t_{k}=0\}
or of a semicircle with center on
\{u_{k}=0\}
. As
x_{0},x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)^{d}
, we deduce that
u_{\min}\leq\gamma_{u_{k}}(y)
for all
k=1,\ldots,d
,
y\in[0,1]
.

The function
y\in[0,1]\mapsto\mathpzc{d}(x_{0},\gamma(y))
is non-decreasing

We can reduce the problem to the case
d=1
. Indeed, for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\gamma_{k}\coloneq(\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{u_{k}})
is the geodesic in dimension 1 connecting
x_{0,k}=\gamma_{k}(0)
and
x_{k}=\gamma_{k}(1)
(see Lemma
H.3
). Furthermore,
\mathpzc{d}(x_{0},\gamma(y))^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathpzc{d}(x_{0,k},\gamma_{k}(y))^{2}
, where, by abuse of notation,
\mathpzc{d}(x_{0,k},\gamma_{k}(y))
denotes the semi-distance in dimension 1 between
x_{0,k}
and
\gamma_{k}(y)
. Hence, if the function
y\in[0,1]\mapsto\mathpzc{d}(x_{0,k},\gamma_{k}(y))
is increasing for all
k
, then
y\in[0,1]\mapsto\mathpzc{d}(x_{0},\gamma(y))
is also increasing.

Until the end of the proof, we therefore concentrate our attention on the case
d=1
.
Let
x_{0},x\in\mathbb{R}\times[u_{\min},+\infty)
, and let
\gamma
and
\tilde{\gamma}
be the geodesic connecting
x_{0}
and
x
, parametrized by
[0,1]
and by arc-length, respectively. Proving that
y\in[0,1]\mapsto\mathpzc{d}(x_{0},\gamma(y))
is increasing is equivalent to proving that
h:y\in[0,l]\mapsto\mathpzc{d}(x_{0},\tilde{\gamma}(y))^{2}
is increasing, where
l\penalty\ =\penalty\ \mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{0},x)
.
We will consider alternatively the cases where the geodesic
\gamma
is a straight line and a semicircle. It suffices to show that
h^{\prime}\geq 0
in both cases.

Proof for straight lines,
d=1
:
See
(
Giard 2025
, Section II.2)
. Using the form of the geodesic given in Lemma
H.2
, we can show that if
\tilde{\gamma}
is a straight line,

h(y)=\ln(\cosh(\sqrt{2}y))\,.

We obtain this formula by deducing from
\tilde{\gamma}(0)=(t_{0},u_{0})
in (
47
) that
c_{1}^{2}=2u_{0}^{2}+\tau^{2}
(the two formulas of (
47
) give the same result). This function is non-decreasing on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Proof for semicircles,
d=1
:
See
(
Giard 2025
, Section II.1)
.
If
\tilde{\gamma}
is a semicircle, then

h(y)=\ln{\left(\frac{\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}+\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}{2\cosh{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}\cosh{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}\right)}+\frac{\sinh^{2}{\left(\sqrt{2}y\right)}}{\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}+\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}\,.

We obtain this formula by deducing from
\tilde{\gamma}(0)=(t_{0},u_{0})
in (
48
) that
c_{3}=t_{0}-\frac{\sqrt{2}\tanh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}{2c_{1}}
and
c_{1}=\pm\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau^{2}+2u_{0}^{2}}\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}
.
Let
y\geq 0
.
We have
h(y)=A(y)+B(y)
with

A(y)=\frac{\sinh^{2}{\left(\sqrt{2}y\right)}}{\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}+\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}\,.

and

B(y)=\ln{\left(\frac{\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}+\cosh^{2}{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}{2\cosh{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}\cosh{\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}}\right)}\,.

Then,

A^{\prime}(y)=\frac{\sqrt{2}\sinh(\sqrt{2}y)\left(\cosh(c_{2}+\sqrt{2}y)+\cosh(\sqrt{2}y)(2+\cosh(c_{2}))\right)}{\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)^{2}}\geq 0

and

B^{\prime}(y)=\frac{\sqrt{2}\sinh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)-\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\right)}{\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)}\,.

If
c_{2}\geq 0
, then as
y\geq 0
,
\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\geq\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)
so
B^{\prime}\geq 0
, hence
\mathpzc{d}(x_{0},\tilde{\gamma}(y))^{2}
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
We now deal with the case
c_{2}<0
.
Remark that
B^{\prime}(y)>0
if and only if
y\notin\left[\frac{-c_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}},\frac{-c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\right]
.
We want to show that
A^{\prime}+B^{\prime}\geq 0
on the interval
\left[\frac{-c_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}},\frac{-c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\right]
.
First, using that for
y\in\left[\frac{-c_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}},\frac{-c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\right]
, we have

\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\leq\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\,,\quad\sinh(\sqrt{2}y)\geq\sinh\left(\frac{-c_{2}}{2}\right)\,,

\cosh(\sqrt{2}y)\geq\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad 2+\cosh(c_{2})\geq 2\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\,,

we obtain a lower bound for
A^{\prime}
on
\left[\frac{-c_{2}}{2\sqrt{2}},\frac{-c_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\right]
:

\displaystyle A^{\prime}(y)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{2}\sinh\left(\frac{-c_{2}}{2}\right)\left(\cosh(c_{2}+\sqrt{2}y)+\cosh(\sqrt{2}y)(2+\cosh(c_{2}))\right)}{2\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{2}\sinh\left(\frac{-c_{2}}{2}\right)\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)(2+\cosh(c_{2}))}{2\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)}\,,

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{2}\sinh\left(\frac{-c_{2}}{2}\right)\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)}{\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}\,.

It follows that

A^{\prime}(y)+B^{\prime}(y)\geq\frac{\sqrt{2}\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\sinh\left(\frac{-c_{2}}{2}\right)\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\sqrt{2}\sinh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)-\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\right)}{\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)}\,.

The positivity of
A^{\prime}+B^{\prime}
follows, since

\displaystyle\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\sinh\left(\frac{-c_{2}}{2}\right)\cosh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)+\sinh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\left(\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)-\cosh^{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\sinh\left(\frac{3c_{2}}{2}+3\sqrt{2}y\right)-2\sinh\left(\frac{3c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)-\sinh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)\,,

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\sinh\left(\frac{c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)-\sinh\left(\frac{3c_{2}}{2}+\sqrt{2}y\right)\right)\,,

\displaystyle\geq 0\,,

where we used
