\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\prod_{l=1}^{d}K_{\rm norm}(x_{l},x_{l}^{\prime})}\left|\sqrt{K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})}^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{b_{k}b_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\right|\,.

The term in the right-hand side has already been dealt with in Lemma
J.5
: (
81
) gives a bound for

\left|\sqrt{K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})}^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{b_{k}b_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\right|=|H_{b_{k}}^{10}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})|\,,

that we denote by
B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}
(note that this bound does not depend on
\Delta
). So

|\mathfrak{g}_{b_{k}b_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k}}K_{\rm norm}(x,x^{\prime})|\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}\,.

Hence
\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(10)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x}\leq\sqrt{d}\sqrt{B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}}e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\leq 3.05\sqrt{d}e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}
.

\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(11)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x,x^{\prime}}
:
We use in the same way as for
B_{H_{b_{k}}^{(10)}}
the notation
B_{H_{b_{k},b_{k}^{\prime}}^{(11)}}
, denoting the bound obtained in Lemma
J.5
for
\mathfrak{g}_{x_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k},b_{k}^{\prime}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{x_{k}^{\prime}}^{-1/2}\sqrt{K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})}^{-1}=|H_{b_{k}b_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})|
(see (
82
)).
For
k\neq l
,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{g}_{b_{k}b_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k}b_{l}^{\prime}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{b_{l}^{\prime}b_{l}^{\prime}}^{-1/2}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\prod_{m=1}^{d}K_{\rm norm}(x_{m},x_{m}^{\prime})}\left|\sqrt{K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})}^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{b_{k}b_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\times\left|\sqrt{K_{\rm norm}(x_{l},x_{l}^{\prime})}^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{b_{l}^{\prime}b_{l}^{\prime}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{l}^{\prime}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{l},x_{l}^{\prime})\right|\,,

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}B_{H_{b_{l}^{\prime}}^{01}}=e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}B_{H_{b_{l}}^{10}}\,.

For
k=l
, we have in the same way

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{g}_{t_{k}t_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{t_{k}t_{k}^{\prime}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{t_{k}^{\prime}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{-1/2}|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{t_{k},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}\,,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{g}_{u_{k}u_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{u_{k}t_{k}^{\prime}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{t_{k}^{\prime}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{-1/2}|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{u_{k},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}\,,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{g}_{u_{k}u_{k}}^{-1/2}\partial_{u_{k}u_{k}^{\prime}}K_{\rm norm}(x_{k},x_{k}^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{u_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{-1/2}|

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{u_{k},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}\,.

So

\displaystyle\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(11)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x,x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\sqrt{\begin{aligned} &d(d-1)B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{4}+d(d-1)B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{4}+2d(d-1)B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}\\
&\>\>+2dB_{H_{t_{k},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}+dB_{H_{t_{k},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}+dB_{H_{u_{k},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}\end{aligned}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq(9.25d+6.95)e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\,.

\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(02)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x^{\prime}}
:

With the same arguments, the terms of
\mathfrak{g}_{x^{\prime}}^{-1/2}H_{2}^{\mathfrak{g}}K_{\rm norm}(x,x^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{x^{\prime}}^{-1/2}
corresponding to derivatives taken in
b_{k}^{\prime},b_{l}^{\prime}
with
k\neq l
can be bounded by
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}B_{H_{b_{l}^{\prime}}^{10}}
.
We bound the terms corresponding to
k=l
with
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}
or
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}
or
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}
, where
B_{H_{b_{k}^{\prime}b_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}
denotes again the bound obtained in Lemma
J.5
.
We get

\displaystyle\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(02)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\sqrt{\begin{aligned} &d(d-1)B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{4}+d(d-1)B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{4}+2d(d-1)B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}\\
&\>\>+2dB_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}+dB_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}+dB_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}\end{aligned}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq(9.25d+45.9)e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\,.

\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(12)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x,x^{\prime}}
:
We denote
M_{b_{k}}=\mathfrak{g}_{b_{k}b_{k}}^{-1/2}\mathfrak{g}_{x^{\prime}}^{-1/2}\partial_{b_{k}}H_{2}^{\mathfrak{g}}K_{\rm norm}(x,x^{\prime})\mathfrak{g}_{x^{\prime}}^{-1/2}
. The terms at positions
b_{l}^{\prime},b_{m}^{\prime}
where
l\neq m
and
l,m\neq k
can be bounded by
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}B_{H_{b_{l}}^{10}}B_{H_{b_{m}}^{10}}
.
The terms at positions
b_{l}^{\prime},b_{k}^{\prime}
or
b_{k}^{\prime},b_{l}^{\prime}
where
l\neq k
can be bounded by
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}B_{H_{b_{l}}^{10}}
or
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}B_{H_{b_{l}}^{10}}
.
The terms at positions
b_{l}^{\prime},b_{m}^{\prime}
where
l=m\neq k
can be bounded by
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{b_{k}}^{10}}B_{H_{b_{l}^{\prime}b_{m}^{\prime}}^{02}}
.
The terms at positions
b_{l}^{\prime},b_{m}^{\prime}
where
l=m=k
can be bounded by
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,b_{k}}}
or
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,b_{k}}}
or
e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}B_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,b_{k}}}
.
Writing
M_{b_{k}}=(m_{b_{l}^{\prime}b_{m}^{\prime}})_{b_{l}^{\prime},b_{m}^{\prime}\in\{t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{d}^{\prime},u_{1}^{\prime},\ldots u_{d}^{\prime}\}}
, we use the following bound for its
2
-norm:
\left\lVert M_{b_{k}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\max_{l}\sqrt{m_{t_{l}^{\prime}t_{l}^{\prime}}^{2}+m_{t_{l}^{\prime}u_{l}^{\prime}}^{2}+m_{u_{l}^{\prime}t_{l}^{\prime}}^{2}+m_{u_{l}^{\prime}u_{l}^{\prime}}^{2}}+\sqrt{\sum_{l\neq m}m_{b_{k}^{\prime}b_{l}^{\prime}}^{2}}
.
It comes that

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{t_{k}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\left(\max\left\{\sqrt{B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}+2B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}+B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}}\,,\,\sqrt{2B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,1}}^{2}+B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,1}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,1}}^{2}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\sqrt{\begin{aligned} &(d^{2}-3d+2)B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}\left(B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{4}+2B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{4}\right)\\
&\>\>+2(d-1)\left(B_{H_{t_{k}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{t_{k}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{t_{k}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{t_{k}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}\right)\end{aligned}}\qquad\right)

and

\displaystyle\left\lVert M_{u_{k}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}\left(\max\left\{\sqrt{B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}+2B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{02}}^{2}}\,,\,\sqrt{2B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,2}}^{2}+B_{H_{t_{k}^{\prime},t_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,2}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}^{\prime},u_{k}^{\prime}}^{12,2}}^{2}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left.\sqrt{\begin{aligned} &(d^{2}-3d+2)B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}\left(B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{4}+2B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{4}\right)\\
&\>\>+2(d-1)\left(B_{H_{u_{k}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}t_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{t_{k}}^{10}}^{2}+B_{H_{u_{k}u_{k}^{\prime}}^{11}}^{2}B_{H_{u_{k}}^{10}}^{2}\right)\end{aligned}}\qquad\right)\,,

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}(170.5+25.78d)\,.

As this last bound is greater than the previous one,

\left\lVert K_{\rm norm}^{(12)}(x,x^{\prime})\right\rVert_{x,x^{\prime}}\leq\sqrt{2d}(170.5+25.78d)e^{-\Delta^{2}/4}
.
∎

Choice of
r,\Delta
for the LPC

Proposition J.1

(
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC,
d=1
)
.

Let
s\geq 2
. Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]
and that
\tau\leq u_{\min}
.
Then
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC (see Definition
5.2
) with parameters
s
,
\Delta(s)=2\sqrt{13.88+\ln(s-1)}
,
r=0.3025
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(0.3025)=0.13139
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(0.3025)=0.04472
.

Proof.

To establish that
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC, we first determine the size of the near regions
r
, giving the constraint on the minimal separation
\Delta
(see Definition
5.2
).

We want to pick
r
such that
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r),\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)
exist and
\frac{1}{64}\min\left\{\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)}{B_{0}}\,,\,\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)}{B_{2}}\right\}
is maximal, using Lemmas
J.3
and
J.2
. This allows us to get the smallest
\Delta
(see item 3 of Definition
5.2
). Graphically, we choose
r=0.3025
(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section VII.1)
). We can take
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(0.3025)=0.13139
and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(0.3025)=0.04472
.
Then

\frac{1}{64}\min\left(\frac{0.04472}{B_{0}},\frac{0.13139}{B_{2}}\right)\geq 0.000204618\eqcolon c_{r}

(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section VII.1)
).

Let
s\geq 2
. The minimal separation
\Delta(s)
must satisfy item 3 of Definition
5.2
. Using Lemma
J.5
, it suffices that

(s-1)\sqrt{2}\,153.05e^{-\frac{\Delta(s)^{2}}{4}}\leq c_{r}\,,

i.e
.
that
\Delta(s)\geq 2\sqrt{c_{\Delta}+\ln(s-1)}
where
c_{\Delta}=13.88\geq\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}153.05}{c_{r}}\right)
(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section VII.1)
).
∎

Proposition J.2

(
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC,
d\geq 1
)
.

Let
s\geq 2
. Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[u_{\min},u_{\max}]^{d}
and that
\tau\leq u_{\min}
.
Then
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC with parameters
s
,
r=\frac{0.3025}{\sqrt{d}}
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)=0.13139
,
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)=\frac{0.0894}{2d}
,
\Delta(s)=2\sqrt{11.9+3\ln(d+6.62)+\ln(s-1)}
.

Proof.

We take Proposition
J.1
as a starting point. We make use of Lemmas
J.2
,
J.4
and
J.6
.
Setting
r=\frac{0.3025}{\sqrt{d}}
, we can take
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)=0.13139
(as in dimension
d=1
) because
d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\mapsto e^{-\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{2d}}|G(r_{0})|
with
r_{0}=0.3025
is non-decreasing.

Furthermore, for
r\leq 0.3025
we have
1-e^{-r^{2}/2}\geq 0.977\frac{r^{2}}{2}
. In fact, for
0<c<1
, denoting

\square_{c}:r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\mapsto 1-e^{-r^{2}/2}-c\frac{r^{2}}{2}\,,

we have
\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\square_{c}(r)=r(e^{-r^{2}/2}-c)
so
\square_{c}
is increasing then decreasing. Then remark that
\square_{c}(0)=0
and
\square_{0.977}(0.3025)\geq 0
.
So we can take
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)=\frac{0.0894}{2d}\leq 0.977\frac{0.3025^{2}}{2d}
(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section VII.2)
).

Moreover,
\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)}{1+B_{00}+B_{10}}\leq\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)}{1+B_{02}+B_{12}}
(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section VII.2)
).
So

\frac{1}{64}\min\left(\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)}{1+B_{00}+B_{10}},\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)}{1+B_{02}+B_{12}}\right)\geq\frac{1}{64}\frac{\varepsilon_{0}(r)}{1+B_{00}+B_{10}}\geq\frac{1}{64}\frac{0.0894}{2d(2+\sqrt{2d})}\eqcolon c_{d,r}\,.

The minimal separation should verify
(s-1)\sqrt{2d}(170.5+25.78d)e^{-\Delta(s)^{2}/4}\leq c_{d,r}
,
i.e
.

\Delta(s)\geq 2\sqrt{\ln\left(\frac{64}{0.0894}\right)+\ln\left((170.5+25.78d)2d(2+\sqrt{2d})\sqrt{2d}\right)+\ln(s-1)}\,.

It suffices that
\Delta(s)\geq 2\sqrt{11.9+3\ln(d+6.62)+\ln(s-1)}
(see
(
Giard 2025
, Section VII.2)
).
∎

Appendix K
Proofs of Section
6

K.1
Proof of Lemma
6.1

We construct
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
in the same way as in Theorem
5.2
, but we do not track the constants and dependence on
d
. We first determine
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
such that
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
can satisfy (
NDSC
). Then we prove that
K_{\rm norm}
verifies the LPC (Definition
5.2
) with
r=r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
and a sufficiently large minimal separation, showing the existence of the non-degenerate certificate
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
.

Choice of
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
:
Given
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>0
, according to Lemma
I.2
it suffices that

1-\frac{B_{02}+\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})/16}{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2}>-1\quad\forall\,x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})

(83)

to have
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>-1
(the existence of this certificate is proven later). Lemma
J.2
gives
B_{02}=\sqrt{4d^{2}+10d}
.
To bound
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2}
, we use Lemma
H.7
. If
\mathpzc{d}(x,x_{j}^{0})<\sqrt{2}
, we have
\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x,x_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq dF(\mathpzc{d}(x,x_{j}^{0}))
where
F
is defined by (
62
) in the appendix. We remark that
F
is continuous on
[0,\sqrt{2}[
and that
F(0)=0
. Furthermore, if
r\leq\frac{0.32}{\sqrt{d}}
we can take
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r)=e^{-\frac{0.32^{2}}{2}}|G(0.32)|
(see Lemma
J.4
). This quantity does not depend on
r
. So there exists
0<r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\leq\frac{0.32}{\sqrt{d}}
(that depends only on
d
) such that (
83
) is satisfied.

LPC and existence of
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
:

It remains to show the existence of
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
, a global non-degenerate certificate of the form (
18
) satisfying Definition
3.2
where the near regions are of radius
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
. We first prove the LPC with this choice of radius, choosing
\Delta
large enough, and then make use of Theorem
5.2
.

The curvature constants
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
are given by Lemma
J.4
.
The separation
\Delta(s)
should satisfy

(s-1)\sqrt{2}\,153.05e^{-\frac{\Delta(s)^{2}}{4}}\leq\frac{1}{64}\min\left(\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})}{B_{0}},\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})}{B_{2}}\right)\,.

Such a
\Delta(s)
exists as
e^{-\frac{\Delta(s)^{2}}{4}}\underset{\Delta(s)\to+\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
. It depends only on
d
(through
\bar{\varepsilon}_{0}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
and
\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
) and
s
. Then
K_{\rm norm}
satisfies the LPC (Definition
5.2
) with these parameters.

Finally, Theorem
5.2
applies.
The minimal separation
\Delta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
follows from Lemma
5.3
. It depends on
d
,
u_{\min}
,
u_{\max}
,
\tau
,
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
,
\Delta(s)
. Under this separation, we can construct a non-degenerate certificate
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
verifying
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}>-1
. We can also construct local non-degenerate certificates
\eta_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
for
j=1,\ldots,s
.

Negative definiteness of
\nabla^{2}\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})
:

Let
j\in\{1,\ldots,s\}
. To show that
\nabla^{2}\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})
is negative-definite, first remark that
\nabla^{2}\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})=D_{2}[\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}](x_{j}^{0})
because
\nabla\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})=0
(equality of the Riemannian and Euclidean hessians, see Definition
5.1
). Then, for
v\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{x_{j}^{0}}=1
, using the control on the near regions (proof of Theorem
5.2
) we deduce that

D_{2}[\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}](x_{j}^{0})[v,v]\leq K_{\rm norm}^{(02)}(x_{j}^{0},x_{j}^{0})[v,v]+\left\lVert D_{2}[\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}](x_{j}^{0})-K_{\rm norm}^{(02)}(x_{j}^{0},x_{j}^{0})\right\rVert_{x_{j}^{0}}\leq-\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}+\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}}{16}=-\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}}{15}\,.

So for all
v\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}\setminus\{0\}
,

v^{T}\nabla^{2}\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})v\leq-\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{x_{j}^{0}}\frac{\bar{\varepsilon}_{2}}{15}<0\,.

K.2
Proof of Theorem
6.1

We begin with a preliminary result.

Lemma K.1
.

Under the hypothesis of Lemma
6.1
,

G_{S_{0}}
is full-rank, where

G_{S_{0}}:(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{s}),(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{s}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d\times s}\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{s}\alpha_{j}\Psi\delta_{x_{j}^{0}}+\sum_{j=1}^{s}\beta_{j}^{T}\nabla_{x}(\Psi\delta_{x_{j}^{0}})\,.

(84)

Moreover,
B\coloneq\max_{k=0,1,2}\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\left\lVert\nabla_{x}^{k}\Psi(\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L},k}<\infty
where

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{x}^{0}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L},0}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Psi\delta_{x}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{x}^{1}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L},1}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\left((\left\lVert\partial_{t_{k}}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})_{k=1,\ldots,d},(\left\lVert\partial_{u_{k}}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})_{k=1,\ldots,d}\right)\right\rVert_{2}\,,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{x}^{2}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L},2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\begin{pmatrix}(\left\lVert\partial_{t_{k}t_{l}}^{2}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})_{1\leq k,l\leq d}&(\left\lVert\partial_{t_{k}u_{l}}^{2}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\\
(\left\lVert\partial_{t_{k}u_{l}}^{2}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})_{1\leq k,l\leq d}&(\left\lVert\partial_{u_{k}u_{l}}^{2}(\Psi\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})_{1\leq k,l\leq d}\end{pmatrix}\right\rVert_{2}\,.

Proof.

G_{S_{0}}
is full-rank:
We used the invertibility of
\Upsilon
(see (
63
)) to construct
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
: we already know that

\sum_{j=1}^{s}\alpha_{j}K_{\rm norm}(x_{j}^{0},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}})+\sum_{j=1}^{s}\beta_{j}^{T}\nabla_{1}K_{\rm norm}(x_{j}^{0},\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}})=0\quad\implies\quad\alpha_{j}=0\,,\;\beta_{j}=0_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\;\forall j=1,\ldots s\,.

B<\infty
:

It can be deduced from the fact that
x\mapsto\Psi\delta_{x}
is
C^{\infty}
on the compact
\mathcal{X}
(Remark
5.1
).
∎

Below we present the main steps of the proof of Theorem
6.1
, adapted from
(
Duval & Peyré 2015
, Lemma 2, Proposition 7)
and
(
Poon 2019
, Section 4.3)
.
We work under the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
: there exists
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
of the form (
18
) that verifies
|\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}|\leq 1
,
|\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x)|=1\iff x\in\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j}
and
\nabla^{2}\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}(x_{j}^{0})\prec 0
for all
j=1,\ldots,s
(Non Degenerate Source Condition, or NDSC).

Dual, noisy and noiseless problems

We denote
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa,b}
the dual problem with regularization
\kappa
and noise
b
.
More precisely:

\underset{p\in\mathbb{L},\left\lVert\Psi^{*}p\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1}{\arg\max}\,\left\langle\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0},p\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}

(
\mathcal{D}_{0,0}
)

is the dual problem of

\underset{\mu\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})}{\arg\min}\,\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\;\text{such that}\;\Psi\mu=\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}\,.

(
\mathcal{P}_{0,0}
)

For
\kappa>0
, the dual problem is

\underset{p\in\mathbb{L},\left\lVert\Psi^{*}p\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1}{\arg\min}\,\left\lVert\frac{y}{\kappa}-p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\,,

(
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa,b}
)

where we observe
y=\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}+b
(
i.e
.

b=\Gamma_{n}
). Its solution is unique. It is the dual problem of

\underset{\mu\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})}{\arg\min}\;\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert y-\Psi\mu\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\kappa\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\,.

(
\mathcal{P}_{\kappa,b}
)

We use the notation
p_{\kappa,b}
for the solution of the dual problem, and
\eta_{\kappa,b}=\Psi^{*}p_{\kappa,b}
.
For
\kappa=0
, we choose the particular dual solution defined by (

p

0
,
0

).

The dual and primal solutions are connected via the subdifferential of the TV-norm: we recall that for
\eta\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
and
\mu\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
,

\eta\in\partial\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\iff\left(\left\lVert\eta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1\,,\>\operatorname{supp}(\mu^{-})\subset\{\eta=-1\}\,,\>\operatorname{supp}(\mu^{+})\subset\{\eta=1\}\right)\,.

Strong duality ensures that for
\mu_{\kappa,b}
a solution of the primal problem,
\eta_{\kappa,b}\in\partial\left\lVert\mu_{\kappa,b}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
. Moreover, for
\kappa>0
we have
p_{\kappa,b}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}(\Psi\mu_{\kappa,b}-\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}-b)
.

Lemma K.2
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
,
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
is the unique solution of (

P

0
,
0

). Moreover,
\eta_{0,0}\coloneq{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}=\Psi^{*}p_{0,0}
where
p_{0,0}
is the solution of (

D

0
,
0

) with minimal norm,
i.e
.

p_{0,0}=\underset{p\in\mathbb{L}}{\arg\min}\left\{\left\lVert p\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\;:\;\Psi^{*}p\in\partial\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\right\}\,.

Proof.

As
{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}\in\partial\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
, we deduce that
p_{0,0}
is a solution of (

D

0
,
0

), linked to any solution
\mu_{0,0}
of (

P

0
,
0

) by
\Psi^{*}p_{0,0}={\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}\in\partial\left\lVert\mu_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
.
So
\operatorname{supp}(\mu_{0,0})\subset\{|{\left.\kern-1.2pt\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\mathcal{X}}}|=1\}=\{x_{j}^{0}\}_{j=1}^{s}
. Using the injectivity of
G_{S_{0}}
(Lemma
K.1
), we have
\mu_{0,0}=\mu_{\omega}^{0}
.

Moreover, as
\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
(which is of the form (
18
)) verifies the NDSC,
(
Duval & Peyré 2015
, Proposition 7)
shows that
p_{0,0}
is of minimal norm.
∎

Lemma K.3

(Convergence of the dual solutions)
.

It holds that
\left\lVert p_{\kappa,0}-p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\underset{\kappa\to 0}{\to}0
.

Moreover,
\left\lVert p_{\kappa,b}-p_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\frac{\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}}{\kappa}
.

Proof.

\left\lVert p_{\kappa,0}-p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\underset{\kappa\to 0}{\to}0
:

Using that
p_{\kappa,0}
is the solution of (

D

κ
,
b

) for
b=0
and
p_{0,0}
is a solution of (

D

0
,
0

), writing
y=\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}
we have

\left\langle y,p_{\kappa,0}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}-\frac{\kappa}{2}\left\lVert p_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert^{2}\geq\left\langle y,p_{0,0}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}-\frac{\kappa}{2}\left\lVert p_{0,0}\right\rVert^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\langle y,p_{0,0}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\geq\left\langle y,p_{\kappa,0}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\,.

We deduce that
\left\lVert p_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}
is bounded by
\left\lVert p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}
. Given
\kappa_{n}\to 0
, as the closed unit ball of a Hilbert space is weakly sequentially compact, we can extract
p_{\kappa_{n_{k}},0}
that converges weakly towards
p^{*}\in\mathbb{L}
.

We show that
p^{*}=p_{0,0}
. We have

\left\langle y,p^{*}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}\geq\left\langle y,p_{0,0}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}
so

\left\langle\mu_{\omega}^{0},\Psi^{*}p^{*}\right\rangle\geq\left\langle\mu_{\omega}^{0},\Psi^{*}p_{0,0}\right\rangle=\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\,.

We also have that
\Psi^{*}
is weakly continuous from
\mathbb{L}
to
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
so

\left\lVert\Psi^{*}p^{*}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert\Psi^{*}p_{\kappa_{n_{k}},0}\right\rVert_{\infty}=1\,,

hence
p^{*}\in\partial\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
. Finally,

\left\lVert p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\geq\lim\inf\left\lVert p_{\kappa_{n_{k}},0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\geq\left\lVert p^{*}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}

as if
h_{n}\rightharpoonup h
in
\mathbb{L}
, then
\left\lVert h\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\leq\lim\inf\left\lVert h_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}
. Hence
p^{*}=p_{0,0}
.

Then
p_{\kappa_{n_{k}},0}\rightarrow p_{0,0}
strongly because
\left\lVert p_{\kappa_{n_{k}},0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\rightarrow\left\lVert p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}
and
p_{\kappa_{n_{k}},0}\rightharpoonup p_{0,0}
.
Finally,
p_{\kappa,0}\underset{n_{k}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}p_{0,0}
strongly because any convergent subsequence of
(p_{\kappa,0})_{\kappa}
has limit
p_{0,0}
.

\left\lVert p_{\kappa,b}-p_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\frac{\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}}{\kappa}
:
The mapping
P:\frac{y}{\kappa}\rightarrow p_{\kappa,b}
is the projection onto a close convex set so it is non expansive: for
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{L}
,

\left\langle h_{1}-P(h_{1}),P(h_{2})-P(h_{1})\right\rangle\leq 0\quad\text{and}\quad\left\langle h_{2}-P(h_{2}),P(h_{1})-P(h_{2})\right\rangle\leq 0\,.

Subtracting the two and applying Cauchy-Schwarz leads to

\left\langle P(h_{1})-P(h_{2}),P(h_{1})-P(h_{2})\right\rangle\leq\left\langle h_{1}-h_{2},P(h_{1})-P(h_{2})\right\rangle\leq\left\lVert h_{1}-h_{2}\right\rVert\left\lVert P(h_{1})-P(h_{2})\right\rVert\,.

Hence

\left\lVert p_{\kappa,b}-p_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\frac{\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}+b-\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}}{\kappa}\right\rVert=\frac{\left\lVert b\right\rVert}{\kappa}\,.

∎

Proposition K.1

(Sparsity of the solution)
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
,
there exists
\tilde{\kappa}_{0}>0
,
\gamma_{0}
such that for all
\kappa\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{0}
and
b
such that
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
, the solution
\mu_{\kappa,b}
of (

P

κ
,
b

) is a discrete measure and has at most 1 particle in each
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
.

Proof.

Using NDSC, the fact that
\mathcal{X}
is compact and that
diam(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{e}))\underset{r_{e}\to 0}{\to}0
, we deduce that there exists
\varepsilon>0
,
r_{\varepsilon}\in]0,r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}]
such that
|\eta_{0,0}(x)|<1-\varepsilon
for all
x\notin\bigcup\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon})
and
\nabla^{2}\eta_{0,0}(x)\prec-\varepsilon I
for all
x\in\bigcup\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon})
.

In light of Lemma
K.3
, we can choose
\tilde{\kappa}_{0}
such that for all
\kappa\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{0}
,
B\left\lVert p_{\kappa,0}-p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
(where
B
is defined in Lemma
K.1
).
For
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
with
\gamma_{0}=\frac{\varepsilon}{4B}
, using Cauchy-Schwarz we get that for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla^{k}\eta_{\kappa,b}(x)-\nabla^{k}\eta_{0,0}(x)\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L},k}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert p_{\kappa,b}-p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\left\lVert\nabla_{x}^{k}\Psi(\delta_{x})\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L},k}\,,

\displaystyle\leq B\left(\frac{\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}}{\kappa}+\left\lVert p_{\kappa,0}-p_{0,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\,.

So
|\eta_{\kappa,b}(x)|<1
for all
x\notin\bigcup\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon})
and
\nabla^{2}\eta_{\kappa,b}(x)
is negative-definite for all
x\in\bigcup\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon})
. Hence there exists at most 1 point in each
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon})
such that
\eta_{\kappa,b}(x)=1
.
As
\eta_{\kappa,b}\in\partial\left\lVert\mu_{\kappa,b}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
, we deduce that
\mu_{\kappa,b}
is a discrete measure and that it has at most 1 particle in each
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon})
.
∎

Lemma K.4
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
6.1
,
there exists
\kappa_{0}>0
,
\gamma_{0}
such that for all
\kappa\leq\kappa_{0}
and
b
such that
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
,
\mu_{\kappa,b}
is a discrete measure and has exactly 1 particle in each
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
.

Proof.

We take Proposition
K.1
as a starting point.
We show that
\mu_{\kappa,b}(\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\varepsilon}))>0
for
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
and
\kappa\leq\kappa_{0}
with
\kappa_{0}\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{0}
small enough.

It suffices to show the weak* convergence of
\mu_{\kappa,b}
towards
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
as
\kappa\to 0
and for
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
(this result is also stated in
(
Duval & Peyré 2015
, Proposition 4)
).
We have

\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}+b-\Psi\mu_{\kappa,b}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\kappa,b}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}

since
\mu_{\kappa,b}
minimizes

J_{\kappa,b}:\mu\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}+b-\Psi\mu\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\kappa\left\lVert\mu\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\,.

Dividing by
\kappa
, it follows that
\left\lVert\mu_{\kappa,b}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
is bounded by
\frac{\gamma_{0}^{2}}{2}\kappa+\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
. So we can extract
\mu_{k}
a weak* convergent subsequence of
\mu_{\kappa,b}
as
\kappa,b\rightarrow 0
with
\left\lVert b\right\rVert\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
. Let
\mu^{*}
be its limit. By the lower semi-continuity of the TV norm for the weak* convergence,
\left\lVert\mu^{*}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\lim\inf\left\lVert\mu_{k}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
. To establish
\mu^{*}=\mu_{\omega}^{0}
, it remains to show that
\Psi\mu^{*}=\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}
(because
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
is the only solution of (

P

0
,
0

), see Lemma
K.2
).
Recall that
\left\lVert p_{\kappa,b}-p_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\frac{\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}}{\kappa}
and that
p_{\kappa,b}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}(\Psi\mu_{\kappa,b}-\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}-b)
, giving
\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\kappa,b}-b-\Psi\hat{\mu}_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\left\lVert b\right\rVert
. As
\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\kappa,0}-\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\leq 2\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}
(because
J_{\kappa,0}(\mu_{\kappa,0})\leq J_{\kappa,0}(\mu_{\omega}^{0})
), it comes

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\kappa,b}-\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\kappa,b}-\Psi\hat{\mu}_{\kappa,0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+\left\lVert\Psi\mu_{\kappa,0}-\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\,,

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+\sqrt{2\kappa\left\lVert\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}}\,.

As
\Psi
is weak* to weak continuous from
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X})
to
\mathbb{L}
, for all
p\in\mathbb{L}
we have

|\left\langle p,\Psi(\mu_{k}-\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}|\to|\left\langle p,\Psi(\mu^{*}-\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}|\,.

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz, we have
|\left\langle p,\Psi(\mu_{k}-\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}|\to 0
from which we conclude that
\left\langle p,\Psi(\mu^{*}-\mu_{\omega}^{0})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}}=0
. So
\Psi\mu^{*}=\Psi\mu_{\omega}^{0}
, and
\mu_{\kappa,b}
converges towards
\mu_{\omega}^{0}
as
\kappa\to 0
and
\left\lVert b\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
for the weak* topology. This concludes the proof.
∎

K.3
Proof of Corollary
6.1

If
n\geq\frac{c_{\kappa}^{2}}{\kappa_{0}^{2}(2\pi)^{d/2}\tau^{d}}
, choosing
\kappa=\frac{c_{\kappa}}{(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}
we have
\kappa\leq\kappa_{0}
. Moreover, it holds that
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
with probability greater than
1-C_{\Gamma}e^{-\left(\frac{\gamma_{0}c_{\kappa}}{C_{\Gamma}}\right)^{2}}
(
c.f
.
Lemma
3.1
where we took
\rho=\frac{\gamma_{0}^{2}c_{\kappa}^{2}}{C_{\Gamma}^{2}}
). Theorem
6.1
applies:
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}=\sum_{j=1}^{s}\omega_{j}^{\star}\delta_{x_{j}^{\star}}
where
\omega_{j}^{\star}>0
and
x_{j}^{\star}\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{near}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}})
for all
j=1,\ldots,s
.

From (
28
), using
\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\gamma_{0}\kappa
and
\left\lVert p_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}\leq\sqrt{2s}
(see Lemma
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle D_{\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}(\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star},\mu_{\omega}^{0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{2}(\gamma_{0}+\left\lVert p_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\kappa}}{2(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})^{2}\,.

(85)

Bound for
|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{\star}|
:
Recalling (
32
), as
\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star}(\mathcal{X}^{far}(r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}))=0
we have

\displaystyle|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{\star}|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert p_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}(2\left\lVert\Gamma_{n}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}+2\kappa\left\lVert p_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})+\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}D_{\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\left(\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa\left\lVert p_{j,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}}(\gamma_{0}+\left\lVert p_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{L}})+\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}D_{\eta_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\left(\mu_{n,\omega}^{\star},\mu_{\omega}^{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c_{\kappa}}{(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}\left(2\sqrt{2}(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})+\frac{1}{2}(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})^{2}\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\right)\,.

The second factor of this product only depends on
\mathcal{X}
,
\tau
and
\mu^{0}
.

Control of
\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})
:

Using (
85
) together with (
31
), we get

\sum_{j=1}^{s}\omega_{j}^{\star}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{g}}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\frac{c_{\kappa}}{2(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})^{2}\,.

To control the proximity between
x_{j}^{0}
and
x_{j}^{\star}
with the semi-distance, we use Lemma
H.5
.
It comes that

\sum_{j=1}^{s}\omega_{j}^{\star}\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\frac{c_{\kappa}}{2(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})^{2}

where
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
only depends on
r_{\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}
.
As
\omega_{j}^{*}\geq\omega_{j}^{0}-|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{*}|
, if
|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{*}|<\omega_{j}^{0}
then

\displaystyle\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{j}^{0}-|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{*}|}\omega_{j}^{*}\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{j}^{0}-|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{*}|}\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\frac{c_{\kappa}}{2(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}\sqrt{n}}(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})^{2}\,.

Recalling (
23
) and the condition
\frac{n}{c_{\kappa}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{\kappa_{0}^{2}(2\pi)^{d/2}\tau^{d}}
, if
c_{\kappa}
is chosen as
c_{\kappa,n}=\underset{n\to+\infty}{o}(\sqrt{n})
, there exists
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
depending on
(c_{\kappa,n})_{n}
,
\mu^{0}
,
\mathcal{X}
,
\tau
and
\tilde{c}_{0}>0
that depends on
\mu^{0}
,
\mathcal{X}
,
\tau
(through
\gamma_{0}
,
\omega_{j}^{0}
,
s

etc
.
) such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
,
|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{*}|<\omega_{j}^{0}
and
\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}_{3,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}{\varepsilon_{2,\textrm{\tiny NDSC}}}\frac{(\gamma_{0}+\sqrt{2s})^{2}}{2(2\pi)^{d/4}\tau^{d/2}(\omega_{j}^{0}-|\omega_{j}^{0}-\omega_{j}^{*}|)}\leq\tilde{c}_{0}
with probability at least
1-C_{\Gamma}e^{-\left(\frac{\gamma_{0}c_{\kappa,n}}{C_{\Gamma}}\right)^{2}}
. Then
\mathpzc{d}(x_{j}^{\star},x_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq\tilde{c}_{0}\frac{c_{\kappa,n}}{\sqrt{n}}
.
